#5144. Core Scientific (CORZ) to Exit Bitcoin Mining by Year-End as Colocation Hits $215 Million

Core Scientific will complete its exit from bitcoin mining by year-end and enter 2027 as a pure-play high-density colocation provider, management said on Thursday’s third-quarter earnings call, capping a pivot that has already cut the company’s mining fleet nearly in half.

Chief Financial Officer Jim Nygaard told investors the company expects to “complete the wind-down during the fourth quarter.” Chief Executive Officer Adam Sullivan put the same end date more bluntly: “We expect to complete our exit from Bitcoin mining by year end.” The comments landed alongside a results release filed with the SEC that shows how far the shift has already gone.

Colocation revenue climbed nearly 60% sequentially to $215.4 million in the quarter ended Sept. 30, the earnings exhibit shows. Digital asset self-mining revenue fell to $12.3 million, down about 79% from $57.4 million a year earlier. Hosted mining for third parties added just $5.2 million. Of $232.9 million in total revenue, colocation now accounts for roughly 92%.

Self-mining posted a $7.5 million gross loss in the quarter. Colocation gross profit was $128.4 million, a 60% margin. Adjusted EBITDA more than tripled sequentially to $133.6 million, with the margin expanding to 57% from 25%.

Nygaard said Core Scientific had nearly 50% fewer miners online at the end of September than at year-end 2025. The company has been repurposing power and floor space under long-term customer agreements for AI and high-performance computing workloads, a process its investor materials describe as continuing “as circumstances allow.”

That path is not unique among public miners. Peers have been selling bitcoin and leasing campuses to AI tenants, and research notes have flagged a widening funding gap between those that can finance data center builds and those that cannot.

Core Scientific is billing for 485 megawatts of colocation capacity as of Sept. 30 — up 90 MW in the quarter — representing more than $700 million in average annualized colocation GAAP revenue. The remaining roughly 100 MW under its CoreWeave buildout is in commissioning and expected to start recognizing revenue over the next few months, Sullivan said.

Beyond CoreWeave’s contracted 590 MW, Core Scientific has 530 MW under an AMD-backed agreement across five campuses, with initial delivery targeted for the first half of 2027 and full delivery by the end of 2028. Together, the two relationships cover about 1.1 GW. Once fully delivered and billing, management expects that portfolio alone to generate $1.8 billion in average annual run-rate revenue and $1.6 billion in net operating income.

Bitcoin is almost gone from the books. Digital assets stood at $2.7 million on Sept. 30, down from $49.7 million three months earlier and $222 million at year-end 2025. Earlier this year, Core Scientific was still adding coins even as it sold into the pivot; that chapter is effectively closed.

The mining exit has already carried a cash cost. A $41.9 million loss on contract termination booked in the second quarter reflects the decision to end a Block/Proto mining chip supply agreement. Liquidity ended the third quarter at about $1.7 billion, including roughly $800 million in cash, after heavy capital spending on AMD sites and the Polaris acquisition.

Sullivan framed the wind-down as simplifying the business toward “more attractive long-term growth opportunities.” Nygaard added that starting 2027 with a “very clean P&L” — one without mining in the numbers — should give investors a clearer look at a true colocation run-rate.

The test now is whether Core Scientific turns the last machines off on schedule in the fourth quarter, and whether the AMD financing package lands in time to keep that clean P&L growing.

#5142. Arkansas sues Kalshi, says its alleged illegal gambling operations cause mental harm

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

The lawsuit adds Arkansas to a growing list of states taking legal action against the prediction market platform.

Arkansas Attorney General Tim Griffin has filed a lawsuit against Kalshi for allegedly operating an illegal gambling platform in the state.

Griffin said in a statement on Wednesday that Kalshi has built a global business that allows users to wager on the outcome of "just about anything."

"It doesn't take a genius to understand that wagering money on whether the Razorbacks win or lose is gambling," Griffin said, adding that such practice is "squarely prohibited" by Arkansas law.

"Although Kalshi insists that its products are not gambling, its actions speak louder than its labels," Griffin added. "Kalshi is not blind that its platform presents real, tangible risks to its users, including excessive wagering, financial losses, depression, and other mental health harms."

Kalshi has previously argued in other lawsuits that its event contracts are derivatives regulated by the Commodity Futures Trading Commission and therefore not subject to state gambling laws.

The Block has reached out to Kalshi for comment on the Arkansas lawsuit.

The lawsuit adds Arkansas to a growing list of states taking legal action against the prediction market platform amid a dispute over whether prediction markets fall under federal derivatives regulations or state gambling laws.

Last week, Utah filed criminal charges against Kalshi, CEO Tarek Mansour, and chief operating officer Luana Lopes Lara, accusing them of illegally promoting online gambling and letting an undercover agent place wagers. The criminal charges came after Utah sued Kalshi and two Susquehanna trading firms, alleging that they operated an illegal gambling business in the state.

Meanwhile, the CFTC has sued at least 10 states, arguing that the states were intruding on its jurisdiction over prediction markets.

"Congress entrusted the CFTC to be the federal regulator with sole responsibility to oversee these markets," CFTC Chair Michael Selig said in a statement. "As I've said before, the CFTC has the expertise and responsibility to defend its exclusive jurisdiction over commodity derivatives, and that’s exactly what we'll do."

#5139. Bitcoin Rebounds After Democratic Midterm Sweep, But Crypto Faces Tougher Fight in Washington

Bitcoin Rebounds After Democratic Midterm Sweep, But Crypto Faces Tougher Fight in Washington

Bitcoin and Ethereum recovered Wednesday after Democrats won control of both houses of Congress in the U.S. midterm elections, despite the results likely making the crypto industry's push for friendlier federal rules more difficult.

The crypto market recovery, in technical terms, appears to be more a natural bounce, and in line with expectations, instead of a trend reversal or a FOMO-driven spike. The overnight selloff also reversed as U.S. stock futures bounced back, making it hard to pin the recovery on politics alone.

Bitcoin briefly hit a six-week low before a bounce that took it back to just above $80,000 once again, above its average price of the last 50 days, which is a solid bullish sign after weeks of bearish correction.

Ethereum, meanwhile, spiked 7% to $2,434, while U.S. stocks on the whole jumped around 1.6%, with the Dow Jones Industrial Average leading the way with a 2.5% rally.

Analysts who spoke to Decrypt said the Democratic sweep had largely been priced into crypto markets before election night. A rebound in Bitcoin therefore says less about investors welcoming the election result than about how much bad news they were already expecting.

The industry has spent more than $300 million supporting crypto-friendly candidates from both parties across the 2024 and 2026 election cycles. About $176 million went toward more than 100 races this year alone.

That spending was aimed partly at getting legislation through Congress that would give digital asset businesses clearer rules. The Clarity Act, a major piece of that effort, would define how federal regulators oversee different types of crypto assets and trading activity.

The bill was in trouble before Tuesday's election. Senators voted 49-50 against advancing it in September, falling short of the 60 votes needed to move past a procedural hurdle. That was not a final vote on the legislation, but it stopped the measure from reaching a full debate.

Negotiations had already run into disputes over restrictions on government officials' crypto business interests and the treatment of rewards paid on stablecoins, tokens designed to hold a fixed value. President Donald Trump's personal and family crypto ventures became a particular point of conflict for Democratic lawmakers.

Democratic control of Congress makes a quick return to the same legislative deal less likely, but it does not automatically undo what the industry secured under Trump. His administration has eased enforcement, established a strategic Bitcoin reserve and overseen the passage of a federal stablecoin law.

The distinction matters more for exchanges, token issuers, and other firms seeking clear legal permissions than for Bitcoin's day-to-day price. Analysts told Decrypt that liquidity, interest rates and investment flows still exert a stronger influence on the cryptocurrency itself.

The broader crypto market has shed roughly $300 billion over the past month, bringing its total value to about $2.67 trillion. Rising borrowing costs and worries about returns on artificial intelligence investments have pushed traders toward less risky assets like bonds or gold.

Major Bitcoin exchange-traded funds, which offer exposure to the asset through traditional brokerage accounts, recorded $215 million in net withdrawals in October. That brought their year-to-date net outflows to $1.84 billion.

The new Congress takes office in January 2027. Unless lawmakers revive the Clarity Act before the current session ends, the industry will need to start a new legislative effort with Democrats controlling both chambers.

#5137. Eric Trump’s American Bitcoin swings to $126 million profit in Q3 on digital asset gains

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

The Q3 profit swing was driven primarily by a $197.34 million gain on digital assets as bitcoin holdings rose to 8,427 BTC.

American Bitcoin swung to profit in the third quarter after recording a $197.3 million gain on digital assets, offsetting weaker mining revenue and higher production costs.

The bitcoin miner, co-founded by Eric Trump, reported net income of $126 million for the three months ended Sept. 30, compared with a $57.2 million net loss in the previous quarter, according to a statement. The company booked a $71.2 million loss on its digital assets in the previous quarter.

American Bitcoin said adjusted EBITDA also turned positive at $220.8 million, from a loss of $45 million in Q2. Mining revenue, however, fell 5.4% quarter over quarter to $63.4 million from $67 million, while bitcoin production declined 4.4% to approximately 891 BTC from 932 BTC.

"These are significant results, particularly in a challenging digital asset market, and they reflect the discipline and long-term conviction behind this business," Eric Trump said in the statement. "But our opportunity extends beyond any single quarter."

On production costs, the company said its expenditure rose 15.3% to approximately $42,100 from $36,500. Gross margin fell to 41% from 49%, while gross profit declined to $25.8 million from $33 million.

American Bitcoin sold a portion of its quarterly bitcoin production to cover operating costs, the company said, while adding about 425 BTC to its reserve and making no purchases during the period. This brought its total holdings to 8,427 BTC at the end of September.

The company's shares closed down 5.6% on Wednesday but climbed 2.4% in extended trading following the earnings release. The stock was down 76% year-to-date.

Meanwhile, other miners also reported substantial digital asset gains. Hut 8 posted $189.5 million in Q3 net income, including $413.8 million in primarily unrealized digital asset gains.

Riot Platforms reported $181.4 million in quarterly revenue, including $116 million from bitcoin mining.

#5135. Bitcoin Price Trades Near $76,000 as Nasdaq Tumbles into Correction

Bitcoin price traded near $76,000 on Thursday afternoon, down more than 5% on the day after sliding through the mid-$75,000s as a Nasdaq-led selloff in chip and AI stocks deepened into a formal correction and forced out more than $1 billion in leveraged bets, Bitcoin Magazine Pro data shows.

The afternoon session extended the morning crash that had already erased overnight gains above $80,000. Bitcoin briefly tested support near $75,400 before stabilizing just under $76,000 into the final hour of U.S. cash trading.

Bitcoin near $75,900 in afternoon trade, on track for its biggest single-day slide since June. The move undid Wednesday’s short-squeeze bounce in a single session. Traders who bought the midterm rebound with borrowed money were the first forced out when stocks cracked at the open, and the bitcoin price never regained the $77,000 area that held as morning support.

Wall Street set the tone. The Nasdaq Composite fell about 5% on Thursday and dropped into correction territory, more than 10% below the record close it set last month, CNBC reported. The S&P 500 lost roughly 4%, and the Dow shed more than 1,600 points.

Semiconductors led the rout. The Philadelphia Semiconductor Index sank about 11%, and the iShares Semiconductor ETF posted its biggest one-day drop since March 2020. Nvidia and Broadcom each fell around 9%, Qualcomm lost about 9% and Tesla dropped about 11%.

Investors are pricing in a slower AI data-center buildout under a Democratic Congress. Traders fear construction pauses and tighter financing for neoclouds, a risk that has weighed on chip and infrastructure names since the midterm results. Analysts told CNBC that the $1.2 trillion of AI capital spending forecast for next year is now being reworked.

Bitcoin has moved with that AI-heavy complex for most of the year. Miners that sold bitcoin to fund data centers are now caught in both trades, so political risk on power and construction hits the same names that used to trade mainly on the bitcoin price.

Forced selling kept the bitcoin price under pressure into the afternoon. Roughly $1.08 billion in leveraged crypto positions were liquidated over 24 hours, including about $954 million in long bets, as futures markets unwound positions built during Wednesday’s short squeeze.

Nearly $900 million of that wipeout came in a roughly four-hour stretch after the U.S. open, turning a stock-led break into a cascade of margin calls. Ether fell below $2,300 as the same leverage flushed out.

Crypto equities followed. Strategy (MSTR), Coinbase (COIN) and Circle (CRCL) each fell more than 10%, and bitcoin miners dropped as much as 20%. Keel Infrastructure (KEEL), which sold its entire bitcoin treasury for $192 million, slid about 20%. CleanSpark (CLSK), Cipher (CIFR) and TeraWulf (WULF) each lost more than 12%.

The next test is Friday’s October jobs report, then October CPI on Nov. 10 and the Fed’s Dec. 9 decision. Policy risk is building too, with House Democrats drafting their own market structure bill ahead of taking the committees in January.

Traders are watching whether the bitcoin price can hold the mid-$75,000 area that served as support on election night and several times since August. A clean break would leave bitcoin with room to run lower, near its weakest levels in months and put the September low back in view.

Options markets have been quiet into the jobs print, but the cash market is not. Volume stayed elevated through the afternoon as stocks and crypto sold together rather than against each other.

At the time of writing, the bitcoin price is near $75,900.

#5133. Samsung Wallet launches USDC accounts on Solana for US Galaxy users

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

Samsung Wallet's Stablecoin Account lets eligible U.S. Galaxy users buy, hold, send and receive USDC on Solana via the app.

Samsung's Stablecoin Account is now live in Samsung Wallet, allowing eligible Galaxy users in the United States to buy, hold and transfer USDC on Solana, according to a statement released Thursday.

Samsung first announced the feature on Oct. 7, with the launch originally expected in the last week of that month.

Around 82 million Galaxy devices in the U.S. are compatible with the service, according to the release. Users must be at least 18 years old, have a Samsung Account and own a compatible device running Android 13 or later. New York residents are excluded.

"For a decade, stablecoins have proved the technology works," Solana Foundation President Lily Liu said. "What they lacked was a way to reach the mainstream."

Users can send USDC to another Samsung Wallet user or a compatible external crypto wallet without paying a Samsung transfer fee. Eligible users can also send money to qualifying bank accounts in more than 60 countries, where recipients may receive local currency, with fees varying by destination.

Licensed stablecoin custodian and infrastructure provider Bastion handles the stablecoin accounts and transfer infrastructure, while Coinbase holds the USDC as its sub-custodian through Coinbase Prime Vault.

Coinbase was already working with Samsung before this launch. In October 2025, Samsung expanded its Coinbase partnership to let U.S. Galaxy users fund Coinbase accounts with Samsung Pay.

Samsung's new Stablecoin Account feature lets users access USDC without installing a separate crypto app or managing private keys themselves. The firm said it also expects to explore online and in-store stablecoin payments, and expansion to additional markets, subject to local regulation.

"Stablecoins have the potential to make moving money around the world faster and easier, and we want Galaxy users to be able to take advantage of that without having to navigate the complexity of traditional crypto tools," said Woncheol Chai, EVP and Head of the Digital Wallet Team at the Mobile eXperience (MX) Business at Samsung Electronics.

#5131. Tech, AI Stocks Split After Democratic Sweep: Microsoft Jumps, Tesla and Nvidia Tank

Tech, AI Stocks Split After Democratic Sweep: Microsoft Jumps, Tesla and Nvidia Tank

Technology stocks delivered a mixed performance Wednesday after Democrats swept the U.S. midterm elections, with investors weighing the prospect of tougher artificial intelligence regulation against expectations that a divided government could limit major policy changes.

The Nasdaq-100 climbed 1.14% to 28,500, recovering from an overnight selloff that briefly pushed index futures down more than 2%.

But beneath the broader market rebound, individual technology stocks told sharply different stories.

Microsoft jumped 6.01% to $552.32, while Google parent Alphabet rose 4.63% to $292.99, according to intraday charts reviewed Wednesday.

While software stocks jumped, hardware stocks corrected to the downside. AI chipmaking giant Nvidia fell 2.19% to $202.17, Tesla dropped 3.61% to $384.10, and Intel lost 5.80% to $57.04.

That said, none has yet shown a heavy bearish trend. Even Nvidia, which is going through the most obvious price correction, remains bullish on the longer trend as the prices test a weak support near the $200 price zone.

Bitcoin also recovered strongly, trading around $80,512, up 4.12% in the day, after an initial election-related selloff.

The conflicting moves reflect the uncertainty over what Democratic control of Congress will mean for technology companies that have benefited from the Donald Trump administration's approach to deregulation.

According to Reuters, investors are particularly concerned about greater scrutiny of AI data centers—the hardware camps of the AI industry—following Democratic victories in states such as Texas and Georgia, where large infrastructure projects have become politically contentious.

The AI sector was already facing questions over enormous infrastructure spending and whether those investments would generate adequate returns.

One of Wednesday's biggest losers was Trump Media & Technology Group, the company behind Truth Social.

Shares plunged 14.29% to $5.10, extending a decline that had been underway long before Democrats secured control of Congress.

The stock had already lost approximately 55% of its value year-to-date before Tuesday's vote.

Technical indicators show traders had been bearish on the stock for months. Trump Media has been in a technical pattern that traders refer to as a “death cross” since March 2025, and has lost nearly 90% of its market cap since its 2024 cap. A death cross happens when the average price of the last 200 days becomes greater than that of the last 50 days, suggesting the asset is losing value way too quickly.

The Nasdaq-100's recovery is encouraging for technology bulls, but the index remains well below its October highs above 31,000. The 28,000 area represents an immediate support zone, while a move back above 29,000 would strengthen the short-term recovery.

Bitcoin's recovery above $80,000 offers another indication that risk appetite has not disappeared entirely.

Still, Wednesday's split between technology winners and losers suggests investors are becoming increasingly selective about their exposure to the AI boom—and the political risks surrounding it.

#5127. Anchorage Digital seeks CFTC swap dealer registration for crypto options business

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

The firm plans to offer European-style bitcoin and ether options with maturities of up to one year if its application is approved.

Institutional crypto platform Anchorage Digital has filed an application to register a swap dealer with the Commodity Futures Trading Commission as it looks to expand its institutional crypto options business.

The application was submitted to the National Futures Association through Anchorage Digital Swap Dealer, LLC, according to a statement shared exclusively with The Block.

Anchorage is now awaiting registration while the NFA reviews its application. CEO and co-founder Nathan McCauley offered no estimate of when that process might finish, but said the company would share further updates as appropriate. "We aren't in a position to speculate on timing," he told The Block.

If the application is approved, McCauley said Anchorage Digital Swap Dealer expects to offer European-style options on bitcoin Bitcoin (BTC) and ether Ethereum (ETH), with maturities ranging up to one year. The firm intends to add more products later, he added, subject to its own approval process and regulatory requirements.

McCauley said that once registration is complete, institutional clients would be able to trade digital asset derivatives with a CFTC-registered counterparty alongside use of Anchorage Digital's existing custody and other services.

Much of Anchorage's existing options business growth has come from public companies holding bitcoin and ether on their balance sheets, the firm said in the statement, while asset managers and funds using options to manage their exposure have also contributed. McCauley would not disclose specific trading volumes or growth figures when asked by The Block, and declined to explain how Anchorage had previously arranged options trades for clients.

"What we can say is that institutional demand for regulated access to digital asset derivatives continues to grow, and establishing a dedicated swap dealer entity is part of how we're building the infrastructure to meet it," he said.

Anchorage Digital Bank, N.A. became the first federally chartered digital asset bank in 2021 and is regulated by the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency. The swap dealer entity would face CFTC requirements for capital, risk management, and margin, using the same framework the Dodd-Frank Act applied to the banks for interest rate, credit, and FX markets, the firm said.

"It will bring our options business under the same federal framework as the dealers they already face," McCauley said in the statement.

In March, Anchorage Digital added collateral management to its Atlas platform, including 24/7 monitoring and automated margin calls for loans, derivatives, and other institutional transactions, The Block reported at the time. It also acquired Securitize's wealth management unit in December to expand its federally regulated crypto services for registered investment advisors.

#5124. What Is x402? How AI Agents Pay With Crypto

What Is x402? How AI Agents Pay With Crypto

Nobody really built the web with payments in mind. Early HTTP specifications included a status code, 402 "Payment Required", that was reserved for a future system of online payments. Then it sat unused for about 30 years. Payments were slow and expensive, and they needed accounts, card details and middlemen who could reverse charges. None of that works for a small payment made by software.

That gap matters more now because AI agents are starting to browse, call APIs and buy services for their users. An agent can't fill in a checkout form or sign up for a monthly plan for every tool it uses. Cloudflare says more than half of internet traffic is now non-human, and it reports seeing a billion HTTP 402 responses on its network every day.

x402 is an attempt to finally give the internet a native way to pay. This guide covers what it is, how it works, who backs it, and whether there's a token involved.

The 402 "Payment Required" code has been in the HTTP standard since the 1990s. It was set aside "for future use", and x402 is the first big effort to actually use it.

x402 is an open payment standard built on top of HTTP, the protocol that moves data around the web. Coinbase launched it in May 2025 with partners including AWS, Anthropic, Circle and NEAR. It lets APIs, apps and AI agents pay for services in stablecoins as part of an ordinary web request.

x402 is not a blockchain and it is not a coin. It's a set of rules: message formats, headers and a request-and-response flow that any server or client can follow. Payments are mostly made in USDC, Circle's dollar-backed stablecoin, with Coinbase's Ethereum layer-2 network Base as the main settlement chain. The protocol works with any chain, though, and it now supports Solana, Polygon, Arbitrum and others, as well as traditional card payments.

On April 2, 2026, the Linux Foundation announced it was launching the x402 Foundation, with Coinbase contributing the protocol and companies including Microsoft, Google, Visa and Mastercard expressing initial support. The new x402 Foundation became fully operational on July 14, 2026, and now stewards the protocol under vendor-neutral governance.

We are excited to introduce x402–a new open source payments protocol built on HTTP rails.

Let’s break it down: pic.twitter.com/9PNI49NK7A

— Coinbase Developer Platform🛡️ (@CoinbaseDev) May 6, 2025

x402 works through a short handshake inside a normal web request:

Most of the onchain work is done by a facilitator, a service that verifies payments and settles them on the blockchain so sellers don't have to run their own crypto setup. On EVM chains, x402 uses EIP-3009, a standard that lets someone transfer tokens by signing a permission instead of sending a transaction themselves. This means the buyer doesn't need to hold ETH for gas.

x402's main pitch is very small payments made by machines. Settlement on Base takes seconds and costs well under a cent, so it makes sense to pay fractions of a cent for a single API call. There's no sign-up, no API key and no subscription. The agent just pays and gets what it asked for.

Adoption has grown quickly. Coinbase released x402 V2 in December 2025, which added wallet-based identity so agents don't have to pay again on every call, automatic service discovery and support for more chains. In May 2026 it added batch settlement, where agents hand sellers signed vouchers that can be settled in bulk. x402 had processed more than 196 million transactions across 12 chains as of November 1, 2026, settling more than $42 million in total, according to Fireblocks, which cited data from tracker agenteconomy.to. Coinbase says more than 97% of onchain agent transactions in Q2 2026 used x402.

It's worth noting, however, that more than half of x402's lifetime transactions came from a two-month frenzy in late 2025, when traders ran automated scripts to mint and sell PING, a token issued through x402, according to Fireblocks. Activity has since settled at a much lower level: in October 2026, x402 processed 5.1 million transactions worth about $274,000.

No. x402 is a protocol, not a cryptocurrency, and it has no official native token. Payments are made in existing stablecoins, mostly USDC. Speculative "x402" tokens have appeared on various chains, but none of them is issued or backed by Coinbase or the x402 Foundation. Be very wary of any token that claims to be the official x402 coin.

To use x402, an agent or app needs a wallet holding a supported stablecoin on a supported network, usually USDC on Base. USDC is widely available on major exchanges including Coinbase, Kraken and Binance. Coinbase lets users send USDC straight to Base. Developers can also use Coinbase Developer Platform wallets or Circle Wallets to give their agents funds, and set spending limits to keep those agents under control.

The x402 Foundation is growing fast. It had 40 member organizations when it became operational in July 2026, and its member list has since grown to 57, including Visa, Mastercard, American Express, PayPal, Google, Shopify, Circle, Stripe and AWS. Michael Hursta became the foundation's executive director on October 1, 2026.

x402 isn't the only option, though. The Agentic Commerce Protocol, an open standard developed by Stripe and OpenAI, and Visa's Trusted Agent Protocol are competing for a role in agent payments, even though Stripe and Visa are also premier members of the x402 Foundation.

Stripe has also co-developed the Machine Payments Protocol with payments blockchain Tempo. Like x402, MPP uses the HTTP 402 code to let agents pay for services, but adds session-based billing so agents can pay as they go rather than settling every request onchain.

Since June 2026, it has also supported x402 payments. The big open question is whether x402's huge transaction counts turn into steady, real commerce. If they do, the payment code that sat unused for 30 years could end up carrying much of the AI economy's payments.

#5119. Canton Coin Gets SEC Nod as Wall Street Tokenization Heats Up

Canton Coin Gets SEC Nod as Wall Street Tokenization Heats Up

The blockchain behind Wall Street’s newest tokenization service just got a regulatory nod for its native token.

The SEC’s Division of Corporation Finance said in a letter dated Tuesday that it views Canton Coin, the native token of the Canton Network, as a “digital commodity” under the agency’s March interpretation of how securities laws apply to crypto.

Agency staff also said they won’t recommend enforcement action if Canton’s validators and application providers mint the token programmatically without registering it.

The relief comes a week after the Depository Trust & Clearing Corp. launched a tokenization service that runs on Canton and DTCC’s own private network, letting firms convert DTC-held stocks and Treasuries into tokens. Morgan Stanley said Tuesday that it was among the first banks to use the service.

SEC staff pointed to two representations from Canton’s lawyers. The first is that the token derives its value from the network’s programmatic operation and from supply and demand, rather than from the managerial efforts of others.

The second is that the network has no “central party.” Canton Coin launched without an initial coin offering or premine, and tokens were initially earned only by running network infrastructure and applications, according to a statement from Canton. Canton’s request letter was dated Oct. 30, four days before staff responded.

The letter has limits, though, as it reflects only staff’s position on enforcement, not a legal conclusion, and rests on the facts Canton presented, the SEC noted.

“Financial institutions need confidence in both the technology they use and the economic model that supports it,” said Yuval Rooz, CEO of Digital Asset, which created the Canton Network.

Canton said the letter is the first relief of its kind since the SEC published its March interpretive release, which found that most crypto assets aren’t securities. It’s also the first since the agency’s August Regulation Crypto Assets proposal.

The decision fits a broader shift since the Clarity Act stalled in the Senate in September, with the SEC and CFTC taking the lead on crypto rules. SEC Chair Paul Atkins said in July the agency was ready to act if Congress didn’t, and the SEC recently proposed rules on how advisers and funds hold crypto.

Digital Asset raised $355 million earlier this year from Wall Street firms and a sovereign wealth fund.

#5116. France backs reporting for self-custodied crypto worth 100K euros or more

The amendment would require taxpayers to report holdings worth at least $112,000, but the measure still needs to survive the budget process.

The French National Assembly approved an amendment that would require taxpayers to report self-custodied crypto holdings totaling at least 100,000 euros ($112,000) to tax authorities.

Amendment I-2701 was adopted on Tuesday in a 150–44 vote, with eight abstentions, according to the Assembly’s official voting record. The threshold would be assessed on Dec. 31 of the relevant tax year.

Separately, the Assembly rejected an amendment that would have exempted gains on crypto payments for goods and services from tax if the payments totaled no more than 1,000 euros ($1,120) during the tax year. The proposal was defeated 69–14, according to the Assembly’s transcript.

The reporting requirement forms part of the 2027 Finance Bill and would need to be retained in the final legislation to become law. The Assembly faces a Nov. 17 deadline to complete its first reading of the bill. Senate debate is scheduled to begin Nov. 20, according to its schedule.

The National Assembly postponed Tuesday’s scheduled vote on the budget’s revenue section for the second time as lawmakers continued debating amendments. The vote was originally scheduled for Oct. 20.

The vote’s postponement itself does not introduce a significant change, but the “likely rejection” of the budget’s revenue section could “result in the rejection of the entire bill in the National Assembly, including provisions adopted by way of amendments,” Faustine Fleuret, global head of public affairs at lending protocol Morpho, told Cointelegraph.

On Friday, the Assembly rejected two proposals that would have applied exit taxes to unrealized crypto gains when taxpayers transferred their tax residence abroad.

Lawmakers also approved an amendment on Oct. 16 that would tax gains from converting crypto into stablecoins. The proposal covers stablecoins classified as electronic money tokens under EU rules and would still need to be retained in the final law.

#5114. Base deploys Denim upgrade on mainnet, replacing Flashblocks with 200ms block times

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

Denim retires Flashblocks, adds millisecond-resolution timestamps, and updates B20 transfer and compliance controls.

Base's fourth mainnet upgrade, Denim, is live, with the Coinbase-incubated Layer 2 now producing a full canonical block every 200 milliseconds, or five per second, the project said in a statement shared with The Block on Thursday.

The upgrade also introduces millisecond-resolution timestamps for smart contracts, alongside new transfer protections and compliance controls for tokens using Base's B20 standard.

Base said the upgrade retires Flashblocks and changes how the pending block tag works, making block production "simpler, more consistent, and more reliable." Each block now follows its parent exactly 200 milliseconds later, with the protocol enforcing the interval rather than relying on the sequencer's clock.

Applications that previously relied on Flashblocks must migrate to standard canonical block subscriptions, according to the statement.

"Base’s north star is to bring the world onchain, starting with trading, financing, and payments," the statement read. "Those use cases need a chain that updates state often and lets builders reason about time precisely."

According to the statement, the upgrade also introduces BaseTime, which gives smart contracts access to Unix timestamps in milliseconds. Ethereum-compatible block.timestamp remains unchanged at whole-second resolution, while new RPC fields provide millisecond timestamps for blocks, transactions, and logs.

Denim also introduces reusable compliance policies through a NOT operator and extends transfer-executor restrictions to direct transfers. It also blocks transfers, mints and seizures that send tokens to the token contract's own address, Base said.

The upgrade follows Azul, Beryl and Cobalt, Base's first three independent upgrades since its 2023 launch. Azul introduced a new client stack and multiproof system, while Beryl brought the B20 token standard. Cobalt added conditional transactions and expanded B20 compliance features.

#5110. US Voters Back AI Data Center Restrictions in At Least 29 Communities

US Voters Back AI Data Center Restrictions in At Least 29 Communities

Voters in at least 29 U.S. jurisdictions backed bans or restrictions on data-center development Tuesday, according to The Washington Post, dealing a new setback to the massive infrastructure buildout behind artificial intelligence.

The measures ranged from outright prohibitions to requirements for additional public approval. Some were nonbinding advisory votes, meaning their passage does not automatically stop construction.

Ballotpedia tracked 41 data-center measures on November 3 ballots across 10 states. Twenty-eight were framed so that a yes vote would curb development, while 13 would permit or support projects, meaning rejecting one of the latter could also count as a defeat for developers.

Ohio produced some of the clearest results. All 10 charter measures backed by the grassroots group Conserve Ohio passed, according to the Post, and more than three-quarters of voters in Hubbard, Ohio, supported a ban on data centers.

The negative sentiment is so big that voters in Granville, Trenton, and Sunbury backed restrictions with margins of roughly 78% to 82%.

In Illinois, nearly 80% of voters in Joliet Township rejected an advisory proposal supporting AI data centers. In Janesville, Wisconsin, more than 60% approved a requirement for another public vote before major development at a former General Motors site linked to a proposed data center.

These facilities house the chips and servers used to train and run AI systems. Their appetite for electricity has turned proposed campuses into local fights over utility bills and grid capacity, while opponents also raise concerns about water use, noise and deals struck without public scrutiny.

AI companies are aware of the growing backlash and are already working to counter it. Some are stepping up lobbying efforts, while others are turning to public relations campaigns to reshape public perception. OpenAI, for instance, has sought to rebrand data centers as “compute campuses,” in an effort to distance the facilities from the negative connotations increasingly associated with them.

The votes arrived alongside victories by politicians promising tougher oversight of data centers. Democrat Gina Hinojosa defeated Republican Gov. Greg Abbott in Texas after campaigning for a moratorium on new projects, while Ohio's governor-elect Amy Acton also supports a pause and stricter rules.

Industry leaders have warned that blanket restrictions would put U.S. AI investment at risk. BlackRock CEO Larry Fink said last month that a broad moratorium could have serious economic consequences. "If we have a moratorium of some sort in this country, we will be in a recession," he said.

#5109. Circle’s ARC token sale lifts earnings as lower yields weigh on USDC income

Circle is looking to payments fees and future staking rewards to diversify revenue as its core stablecoin business faces pressure from lower yields and growing competition.

Circle’s expansion beyond stablecoins supplied the biggest boost to its third-quarter earnings, as the company recognized $182 million in revenue from its ARC token presale, helping offset falling income from the reserves backing its flagship stablecoin, USDC.

The company reported diluted earnings per share of $0.84, up from $0.64 a year earlier and double analysts’ expectations of $0.42. Total revenue and reserve income rose 17% to $865 million. Without the ARC token sale, however, revenue would have fallen from a year earlier.

Circle shares were down 0.84% at $74.59 at 4:05 pm UTC on Nov. 4, according to Yahoo Finance.

The results capture a transition for Circle. Its established stablecoin business earns returns on the reserves backing USDC, tying income to interest rates and the amount of the token outstanding. Its newer businesses aim to earn from the payments and financial activity those digital dollars support.

Circle launched its payments network in May 2025 to connect financial institutions settling cross-border payments with stablecoins. Arc, its blockchain for financial applications, went live on public mainnet on Sept. 16, 2026. Its native token, ARC, is intended to support network security and governance as Arc moves toward proof of stake in 2027, while transaction fees are paid in USDC.

That contribution arrived as lower reserve yields outweighed growth in USDC balances. Average circulation increased 8% year over year, but the reserve return fell 61 basis points to 3.5%. Reserve income declined 8% to $657.6 million.

Interest rates were lower than a year earlier, despite a Federal Reserve rate hike in September. The Fed raised its benchmark range by a quarter percentage point to 3.75%–4% in September, although that remained below the 4%–4.25% range set a year earlier. Circle’s reserve return also edged up from 3.48% in the second quarter, but the earnings pressure reflected the lower year-over-year yield.

USDC ended September at $73.9 billion in circulation, while its share of the stablecoin market fell 1.83 percentage points to 27%.

Competition is also expanding. Open Standard’s Open USD was live on Solana by Sept. 30, with Coinbase, Mastercard, Shopify, Stripe and Visa as founding partners. The stablecoin model distributes reserve earnings to partners after a management fee, giving businesses another option for accessing stablecoins and sharing their economics.

Related: ARK buys $17M in Coinbase, Circle shares while trimming Bullish

ARC changed Circle’s earnings picture. Excluding ARC presale revenue, total revenue and reserve income were $683 million, down roughly 8% year over year. Reported adjusted earnings before interest, taxes, depreciation and amortization rose 85% to $317 million. Without the ARC contribution, adjusted EBITDA was approximately $135 million, down 21% from $171 million a year earlier.

Circle anticipates staking rewards after Arc transitions to proof of stake, which it targets for 2027. That would create another source of revenue, although it remains dependent on the network’s development.

Circle is also beginning to charge for some payments-network activity. Chief Financial Officer Jeremy Fox-Geen said on the earnings call that the company expected material CPN revenue in 2027. CEO Jeremy Allaire described Circle’s strategy as building connected networks, with growth in stablecoins, payments and blockchain applications reinforcing one another.

“This is not just about payments, and it is not just about digital dollars. It is about multiple compounding flywheels on one platform. Every new member, every new builder, and every new asset makes the whole platform more valuable,” Allaire said.

The company is also adjusting its workforce size. The earnings disclosures included a reduction affecting approximately 5% of employees, which Circle said was intended to align its organization with its operating model and strategic priorities.

Midterm election results appeared to generate short-term optimism, but analysts say they haven't resolved macroeconomic uncertainties.

About $879.92 million in positions were liquidated over a four-hour period, including $818.90 million in longs and $61.02 million in shorts, according to CoinGlass. Total liquidations over the past 24 hours reached nearly $1.1 billion, affecting nearly 211,000 traders.

It should be noted that liquidation figures are typically based on publicly available data, which may significantly understate the extent of market liquidations.

However, those factors proved short-lived as broader macroeconomic pressures persisted.

"It's probably a combination of all of them in some way. What matters is that all of them seemed to be so short-lived that it actually didn't really matter," said Jasper De Maere, an OTC trader at Wintermute. "Trump is still the president for two years, the Middle East is still escalating, inflation is elevated into a strong U.S. economy."

Beyond the immediate market pressures, the election results may also complicate the outlook for crypto regulation, potentially tempering some of the optimism that helped drive Wednesday's rebound.

Paul Howard, senior director at Wincent, said the Democrats' strengthened position in Congress could make bipartisan cooperation necessary for meaningful progress on crypto legislation over the next two years. With the Clarity Act's prospects uncertain, Howard expects the existing regulatory framework to largely remain intact until the next election cycle.

Ryan Kirkley, CEO of Global Settlement Network, said the election could delay crypto legislation but wouldn't fundamentally change longer-term institutional demand for digital assets.

"Investors are still focused on liquidity, interest rates and the broader economy, and those factors will continue to influence the market regardless of which party controls Congress," Kirkley said.

If the weakness continues, Wintermute identified $73,000 and $68,000 as the next levels to watch for bitcoin. The firm said it would need to see bitcoin convincingly reclaim and hold $80,000 before turning more positive.

Riot estimates the project’s total capital costs at $5.5 billion, financed primarily through debt backed by lease payments, although financing has not yet been secured. The first phase is expected to be delivered in September 2027, with rental payments beginning within 60 days of delivery.

#5104. US Bank Regulator Issues First AI Framework as Banks Battle Rogue Agents

US Bank Regulator Issues First AI Framework as Banks Battle Rogue Agents

The U.S. Office of the Comptroller of the Currency issued its first supervisory framework for artificial intelligence in banking today, telling examiners to focus less on how powerful an AI system is and more on what happens when it makes a mistake.

The OCC's framework addresses that very concern, although the regulator has not linked its issuance to those specific incidents.

In a section covering operational resilience, the agency warns that external agents operated by customers, fintechs, or other businesses could create risks for banks even when the banks don't control those systems.

It suggests examiners consider measures including identity verification, delegated permissions, transaction limits, monitoring, and the ability to suspend or revoke access.

The guidelines aim to tackle the dilemma of how to let customers use AI assistants to manage their finances without giving autonomous software unrestricted access to accounts that banks are already facing.

The new OCC guidance arrives less than two weeks after reports that major banks were discovering unauthorized third-party AI agents operating inside customer accounts, sometimes taking actions that appeared to go beyond users' instructions.

Morgan Stanley was blocking outside agents, while JPMorgan, Bank of America, and Citi were restricting access, according to an Axios report on October 22. Bank executives described the situation as "horrifying," raising questions about who controls an autonomous agent once it has access to someone's money.

The OCC's framework makes a distinction between an AI system's technical sophistication and the damage it could cause.

A simple agent authorized to initiate financial transactions may present greater risk than a more advanced model limited to writing internal documents, the regulator said.

Under the 22-page framework, examiners can consider the consequences of failure, whether an action can be reversed, and how much control humans retain over the system.

The agency outlines four illustrative risk tiers. Fully autonomous payment agents and real-time credit decision systems fall into the highest category, while basic internal productivity tools occupy the lowest.

Higher-risk systems may warrant tighter restrictions on autonomy, independent testing, and human approval before consequential actions can proceed.

The framework also addresses AI systems that continue operating while making harmful decisions. Suggested safeguards include circuit breakers, rate limits, restricted permissions, and emergency shutdown controls.

Those considerations are especially relevant to the outside agents described in Axios's earlier reporting, where banks struggled to distinguish legitimate customer-authorized software from systems operating beyond their intended permissions.

The OCC stressed that the document does not create new regulatory requirements or enforceable standards for banks.

Instead, it gives examiners a way to evaluate AI within existing operational, compliance, and cybersecurity risk categories. Banks are not required to establish a separate AI risk category.

The document uses the mandatory term "Super Intelligence," which the Donald Trump administration insists on using in place of “artificial intelligence,” but its scope includes conventional AI and “autonomous agents” rather than only hypothetical systems with intelligence beyond human capabilities.

The framework covers AI used in payments, fraud detection, customer service, and other banking operations. It also extends its risk considerations to external AI agents connecting through bank channels.

The OCC said it intends to update the guidance as the technology develops. For now, its illustrative risk scale puts autonomous payment agents in the highest-risk category, regardless of how well they perform in testing.

#5102. Hyperliquid Strategies files to become CFTC-registered FCM, posts $13.3 million in quaterly revenue

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

The filing would let the HYPE treasury firm carry U.S. client accounts for HIP-3 perpetual futures markets, pending regulatory approval.

Nasdaq-listed Hyperliquid Strategies (PURR) reported $13.3 million in revenue for the quarter ended Sept. 30, exceeding analyst estimates of $10.6 million to $12 million, depending on the data provider.

The company posted $620.6 million in net income, largely driven by $803 million in unrealized gains on its Hyperliquid (HYPE) token holdings, partially offset by a $172.3 million income tax provision.

Perhaps most notably, Hyperliquid Strategies filed with the CFTC to become a futures commission merchant (FCM), which would allow the firm to carry U.S. client accounts for HIP-3 markets.

"Hyperliquid recently announced its intention to add additional deployer functionalities under the name HIP-3," the firm said in a release. "These optional additional features would allow deployers to build and launch permissioned markets on Hyperliquid's onchain infrastructure. Independent operators would run their own market in a compliant manner, while the chain underneath would stay a neutral infrastructure layer."

The FCM, which is subject to regulatory approval, would allow Hyperliquid Strategies to "play a role" in bringing perpetual futures to U.S. customers within a regulated framework.

"There can be no assurance as to the timing or outcome of the application or regulatory approval of permissioned HIP-3 markets," the firm said.

This comes as Hyperliquid's underlying infrastructure moves closer to the U.S. market. NYSE parent Intercontinental Exchange agreed last week to partner with TradeXYZ to bring Hyperliquid-based perp futures markets to U.S. traders. ICE would operate the exchange and clearing infrastructure, with TradeXYZ designing the markets.

Earlier this year, President Donald Trump said his administration was working to bring Hyperliquid into the U.S. "in a fully compliant and legal fashion."

#5101. Trump-linked tokens lead crypto losses after Democrats sweep in the US midterms

The official Trump and World Liberty Financial tokens were the two biggest losers among the top 100 cryptocurrencies on Wednesday, even as Bitcoin and Ether rallied.

Two crypto tokens linked to US President Donald Trump led losses among the top 100 digital assets on Wednesday following the US midterm elections, raising questions about their future as the broader crypto market rallied.

Official Trump (TRUMP) fell 17% over 24 hours to $1.33, while World Liberty Financial (WLFI) dropped over 7% to $0.047, according to CoinMarketCap data from around 5:32 pm UTC. TRUMP is the president’s official memecoin, launched in January 2025, while WLFI is the governance token of World Liberty Financial, a Trump family-backed decentralized finance (DeFi) project launched in 2024.

The declines followed a wave of Democratic victories, including surprise wins in Texas, in Tuesday’s midterm elections, with the party winning control of both the House and Senate from Republicans.

The results could not only make it harder for Trump to push forward his crypto plans but also bring more scrutiny to his family’s crypto businesses. Analysts say the losses also raise questions about whether political sentiment alone can fuel demand for Trump-linked tokens.

Bitcoin (BTC) rose more than 4% over 24 hours to around $80,500, while Ether (ETH) climbed 6% to $2,433, according to CoinMarketCap data. Other major cryptocurrencies also posted gains, with BNB rising 3%, XRP climbing 2.6% and Solana (SOL) gaining 2.5%. The overall crypto market rose 3.4% to $2.69 trillion.

Meanwhile, the losses extended to other Trump family-linked tokens, such as the MELANIA memecoin, associated with First Lady Melania Trump. MELANIA dropped nearly 13% over 24 hours to around $0.066.

A separate token named after Vice President JD Vance (VANCE) also dropped nearly 12%, although it has no confirmed official connection to the vice president.

The losses were further reflected across the broader political memecoin market, with the sector’s total market capitalization falling more than 16% on the day to $477 million. Trading volume jumped 123% to $437 million, per CoinMarketCap data.

Related: US midterm elections 2026 as it happened: Democrats win control of Congress

Benjamin Sarquis Peillard, founder and CEO of Cap, a DeFi platform focused on stablecoin yields, told Cointelegraph that investors appear to be treating politically linked tokens differently from other cryptocurrencies.

“Bitcoin and other established assets respond to a combination of liquidity, institutional flows and macroeconomic conditions, while politically linked tokens can be more sensitive to shifts in expectations around Washington,” he said. “Even as the broader market recovers, those tokens may face selling pressure if investors begin reassessing their risk exposure.”

And while the selloff could be indicative of a short-term reaction to the election results, Peillard said the “longer-term question is what supports demand for these tokens beyond their association with a political figure.”

Beyond the market reaction, the election results also raise questions about the future of US crypto legislation, including the currently stalled Digital Asset Market Clarity Act, commonly referred to as the CLARITY Act.

The bill, which aims to establish a regulatory framework for digital assets, failed to advance in the Senate earlier this year after falling short of the 60 votes required. Democrats were pushing for stronger ethical provisions and safeguards against conflicts of interest involving Trump’s crypto ventures — four Republicans also opposed advancing the legislation.

“The midterm results introduce a new layer of regulatory uncertainty, particularly around the pace and direction of digital asset legislation,” Peillard said. “For politically linked tokens, that uncertainty can have a more immediate impact because investor sentiment is partly tied to expectations around the political environment.”

While the crypto sector continues to have support — and opposition — from lawmakers in both parties, it remains to be seen how the election results will affect crypto legislation.

Peillard said that for the broader industry, the “more important question is whether Congress can establish a durable regulatory framework that provides clarity regardless of which party holds power.”

#5097. Coinbase Says 99% of Customer Crypto Is Offline and Out of Hackers’ Reach

Coinbase Says 99% of Customer Crypto Is Offline and Out of Hackers’ Reach

Coinbase is moving to reassure customers that what happened at Binance can’t easily happen there.

In a blog post Wednesday, the U.S. exchange said it wasn’t affected by last week’s hack of Binance, in which attackers drained about $6.4 billion from the rival exchange’s hot wallets.

Coinbase said it keeps more than 99% of customer assets in cold storage, meaning offline wallets that can’t be reached over the internet. It moves funds into internet-connected hot wallets only as customers need them.

The Binance incident “has understandably left customers asking” how Coinbase guards against a similar attack, the post said.

Coinbase laid out several layers of protection. Private keys for its cold wallets are split into pieces and never fully reassembled, even when signing transactions, and no single employee has standing access to the hot-wallet signing service. Strict limits cap how fast funds can move from cold to hot wallets, and unusual withdrawal spikes are held until staff review them. The exchange can also trigger “kill switches” that take signing keys offline entirely.

The company said it assumes employees will be targeted by phishing, so its controls are designed so that a stolen password, session or device can’t be used on its own, and sensitive actions require multiple approvals.

That focus on insiders carries weight for Coinbase. In May 2025, it disclosed that criminals had bribed overseas customer support agents to steal personal data on less than 1% of its monthly transacting users, then demanded a $20 million ransom that Coinbase refused to pay.

Last week, Coinbase said a security incident at analytics vendor Amplitude exposed contact data for a “small subset” of customers, though funds weren’t affected.

Coinbase also said customer assets are held 1:1 and aren’t lent out unless customers direct it to, and it reported $8.6 billion in cash and cash equivalents and $377 billion in assets on its platform as of Sept. 30. It flagged artificial intelligence as a fast-changing threat and pledged further security investment.

The Binance theft, which the FBI has attributed to North Korea’s TraderTraitor group, has rattled the industry. Blockchain analytics firm Elliptic found that the laundering spanned about 12,000 wallets in under four days, while stablecoin issuers froze about 44% of the haul.

Separately, Brazil’s Mercado Bitcoin halted withdrawals after a $40 million hack, and French bank Crédit Mutuel Arkéa recently confirmed a cyberattack that led to a $133 million crypto theft.

#5096. How Aussie Bitcoiners can avoid 2027’s shock CGT hike

From setting up an SMSF or a company, to just leaving the country entirely, here are some strategies Australian Bitcoiners can employ to deal with 2027’s massive CGT hike.

The Australian government’s decision to scrap the 50% capital gains discount for all investments in the May budget came as a big shock for crypto owners. The new scheme effectively doubles the taxable component of a gain, minus a small discount for inflation.

But there are legal ways to minimize the amount of CGT you’ll have to pay — if you start prepping now.

Some Aussie crypto holders are taking drastic measures — like Finder founder and crypto king Fred Schebesta who is relocating to Singapore which offers 0% CGT, stating the tax changes were a “killer blow.”But there are less dramatic methods to reduce your CGT bills including setting up a self managed super fund (SMSF), a company to trade crypto at the company tax rate, or to just cross your fingers and hope the opposition will reverse the changes after the next election.

Since 1999, Aussie asset owners have only paid tax on half of their capital gain. From July 1, 2027 they’ll pay their full income tax rate — which means 30% CGT if your income and gains are between A$45,000 and $135,000, 37% for the portion between $135,000 and $190,000 and 47% above that. (All figures in this story are in Aussie dollars)

Adding insult to injury, low-income earners who aren’t on welfare like students and retirees will pay a minimum 30% CGT rate no matter how little the gain.

The only discount now available accounts for the effect of inflation on your cost basis. So if you buy a house for $1 million in 2027 and sell it for $1.5 million after 10 years, with inflation at 3%, you won’t have to pay tax on the $344,000 or so attributed to inflation.

“The challenge for cryptocurrency is that the CGT changes, indexation really helps slower growing assets not fast ones,” explains Andrea Yuen, Swyftx CEO.

So if you bought Bitcoin for $10,000 and sell it ten years later for $100,000, the inflation discount is only around $3,439. Under the old system you would pay tax on $45,000 of gains. Under the new system you’ll pay tax on $86,561 of gains.

Stockspot analyst Chris Bryki compared how ten different asset classes would fare under the new rules. “Crypto sits at the bottom because almost its entire return comes from capital growth,” he wrote in The Australian newspaper.

Here are a few different strategies to help crypto holders deal with the changes, ranging from little to no effort, to restructuring your entire life.

Any gain you’ve made before July 1 2027 will be taxed under the existing 50% discount scheme. So your best bet is to hope a raging bull market pushes the value of your portfolio as high as possible before that date.You should faithfully record the value of every crypto asset you have that day, as any gain from that value will be taxed at the higher rate.

Ben Simpson, founder of crypto advisory service Collective Shift, says Bitcoin’s performance since mid-year in a challenging macro environment bodes well for further gains before July.

But Simpson (who points out he’s not a financial advisor) adds, “I probably wouldn’t be betting the farm on the fact that we’re gonna have a huge bull run”

Yuen notes that prices aren’t within your control.  “A strategy isn’t a strategy if you’re just sort of hoping,” she says.

Both the official opposition The Coalition, and the insurgent opposition One Nation, have promised to reverse the changes. Crypto voters who feel strongly enough about this single issue, could vote for a change in government.However, the ruling ALP holds 94 out of 150 seats in the House of Representatives and will be difficult to dislodge from office. If the government does change — and the legislation can pass the Senate in time — it’s technically possible the changes could be reversed before anyone has to file taxes from July 1 2028.

But it’s probably more likely that any change to the system would occur after that date. Adrian Forza, director of Crypto Tax Australia, says this would make calculating gains very difficult.

Australian retirement funds are called superannuation funds, and once you are over 60 you can access your investments tax-free — even as they keep growing.Unfortunately none of the retail super funds allow clients to invest in crypto, so if you want to protect your crypto gains using this method, you’d have to set up an SMSF. About a third of Simpson’s high net worth clients invest in crypto via SMSFs.  “Buying Bitcoin and accumulating it there is a super solid idea,” he says, although he cautions against adding many altcoins. “You’re risking your retirement, which is an ultra long-term hold, on short-term altcoins,” he says.

Australian super fund AMP has invested in Bitcoin — but doesn’t allow its clients to do so.

The big drawback with SMSFs — explains the 27-year-old founder — is that younger crypto investors can’t access the money for decades.

Realistically then, it’s a strategy more suited to wealthier investors aged over 50, who have a shorter period to wait.

Related: Older investors are risking everything for a crypto-funded retirement

The government has also made running an SMSF a pain in the backside, with lots of compliance paperwork and audits, as well as a bunch of unavoidable fees that make it prohibitive for smaller balances. Annual costs typically range between $2,000 and $4,000, although online providers like Stake can reduce total annual costs to around $1,330, as long as you only buy Bitcoin or Ether ETFs.

You also have to sell any crypto and pay tax on it before you can rebuy it with your SMSF, and there are annual contribution limits to factor in, although Forza says you should be able to get almost $400,000 in there with a bit of creativity.

Keep an eye out in December for a detailed report on how to set up a crypto SMSF.

A strategy some billionaires employ to avoid paying tax is by borrowing against their assets, as repaying the loan often works out cheaper than paying tax on a sale.

Bitcoiners can now access a range of lenders, including BlockEarner, Vield and Swyftx, that let you employ a similar strategy by borrowing against your BTC as collateral.

“We like to bring that strategy to the masses, not just to billionaires,” laughs Yuen.

But it can be risky if you don’t have a billionaire’s deep pockets. Borrowers face high interest rates, relatively short loan periods (typically 12 months), and risk losing their Bitcoin if the market moves against them. You can’t borrow against the full value either — Swyftx has a loan-to-value ratio around 40%.”If your collateral drops below a certain amount, there will be a margin call,” she says.  “I would say it’s a bet on a price going up. I think you have to have a view that crypto will go up — or at least not go down so that you get margin-called... Otherwise, a crypto-backed loan wouldn’t make sense for you.”

Simpson doesn’t recommend this strategy due to the risks, but says it can pay off for some people. For example if you borrow at 10% interest, and Bitcoin compounds by 33% a year (as it has done in the past) you’d end up much better off than paying CGT.

But he warns it’s a leveraged bet that is best suited to sophisticated investors.

Bitcoin is down 28% over the past year, so anyone borrowing a year ago would have faced margin calls and possible liquidation as well as interest costs.

Borrowing against Bitcoin for a house deposit has become a more popular strategy. (BlockEarner)

Forza cautions that borrowing crypto can also be a CGT event in some circumstances, such as borrowing in DeFi. If you deposit ETH to Aave, it gets automatically swapped to WETH which  is a taxable event, and when you withdraw the ETH it’s another taxable event.

Borrowing from centralized lenders like BlockEarner or Swyftx is currently a grey area, and the ATO has not clarified if it’s a taxable event.”If you’re talking about [borrowing] ten grand fine. But if you’ve got three to five Bitcoin, I wouldn’t risk it because if it goes against you then you’re going to be paying tax.””Vield is the only one in Australia that’s got a private ruling that says their product is not a taxable event. No one else has got one.”Swftxy confirmed it has not got a private ruling and said clients should seek tax advice, while BlockEarner did not respond to a request for comment.

In part two of our Aussie CGT special, coming later this week, we’ll bring you the final four strategies to minimize your crypto tax bills, including setting up a company or leaving the country entirely.

#5092. Google Tells Schools They Can Reject AI—But They'll Lose Search, Too

Google Tells Schools They Can Reject AI—But They'll Lose Search, Too

Google is giving schools more freedom to reject artificial intelligence—provided they're also willing to give up Google Search, which is arguably what everybody in the world uses Google for.

The company announced Tuesday that its AI products will be disabled by default for K-12 student accounts unless administrators explicitly enable them. But schools that want to ban AI entirely will also have to disable Google's search engine, a tool millions of students rely on for everyday research.

Chris Phillips, Google's vice president and general manager of education, tried to justify the reasoning during a meeting with educators.

“Search and AI are deeply integrated,” he said according to Education Week. Schools uncomfortable with AI can choose not to enable Search at all.

Google says administrators will be able to activate or disable Gemini, Gemini Notebook, Gemini in Classroom, and Search individually. That means schools can block the standalone chatbots while keeping Search available, but they cannot remove AI from Search itself.

For teachers who want students to research sources without receiving AI-generated answers, the company is effectively offering two options: accept its AI-powered search or find another search engine.

The announcement is already drawing accusations that Google is using its dominance in education to push a technology many schools are still trying to understand, regulate or restrict.

Google says the changes are intended to address concerns that AI chatbots can undermine learning by giving students ready-made answers instead of encouraging them to solve problems themselves.

Beginning in early 2027, schools that enable Search will be offered versions with conventional AI features, such as AI Overviews and AI Mode, or the education-focused Guided Learning experience, according to The New York Times.

Neither option provides the traditional search experience without generative AI.

New York City Mayor Zohran Mamdani accused the company of “trying to use its monopoly power to force AI into our public schools,” according to CBS News.

Former Federal Trade Commission chair Lina Khan was equally critical, accusing Google of using its search monopoly to coerce schools into adopting AI.

The criticism comes as some of America's largest school districts are actively restricting generative AI. New York City, for example, imposed a one-year moratorium on student-facing generative AI through eighth grade, covering nearly 600,000 students, while Los Angeles has also restricted the technology.

Los Angeles officials had specifically hoped to preserve Google Search while removing AI-generated summaries. Under the newly announced controls, that won't be an option.

Google's position also arrives amid continuing antitrust scrutiny. A federal court ruled in 2024 that Google unlawfully monopolized the U.S. search market, and the company has faced separate legal challenges over its decision to integrate AI summaries into search results.

School administrators are expected to receive the new controls in early 2027. They will be able to decide which AI products students can access, and even apply different settings by grade level. A Google Search option without AI, however, is not among the choices.

That said, for schools that want to, ahem, unofficially ban AI without losing Google search, our guide may provide a quick solution. It takes 5 minutes and lasts forever.

#5086. ChatGPT Is Getting a GIF Maker Powered by OpenAI's Unannounced Images 2.6 Model

ChatGPT Is Getting a GIF Maker Powered by OpenAI's Unannounced Images 2.6 Model

The internet may soon have an unlimited supply of AI-generated dancing cats, courtesy of ChatGPT.

OpenAI is rolling out a feature that lets users generate animated GIFs directly inside ChatGPT, according to The Verge. The feature reportedly comes powered by its new image-generation model called Images 2.6, which OpenAI has yet to formally introduce through its usual product announcements.

Users can describe an animation in plain English or upload an existing photo and ask ChatGPT to bring it to life. Once the GIF is generated, they can adjust its playback speed, inspect individual frames, make edits, and share the finished animation.

OpenAI demonstrated the capability with a simple request: “can you make a gif of a cat dancing?” The resulting interface reportedly includes controls to change animation speed and examine the GIF frame by frame.

We tried generating GIFs using ChatGPT, but the feature was not available on our accounts at the time of testing. In our interaction, the model generates a series of frames, but not the animation.

The rollout appears to be gradual, so users may have to keep asking for their dancing cats a little longer.

The GIF maker is reportedly part of a broader Images 2.6 update that promises faster image generation. The Verge reports that OpenAI is bringing the model to ChatGPT and Codex users across web, desktop, and mobile platforms.

Developers are also getting two new API models, GPT-Image-2.6 Flare and GPT-Image-2.6 Sunburst, designed for faster generation and higher-quality results, respectively. OpenAI has not yet published a standalone announcement detailing the new model's capabilities, and we could not independently test its performance.

The update follows the September release of ChatGPT Images 2.5, which introduced sharper details, more precise editing, and generation speeds up to 50% faster than its predecessor.

In our previous testing, Images 2.5 went head-to-head with Google's Nano Banana 2 across six categories, with each model winning three. OpenAI's model delivered particularly impressive illustration quality and improved on the oversharpening problems that affected earlier versions, although it still made mistakes when rendering text.

The move into GIF generation also comes months after OpenAI shut down Sora, its standalone AI video-generation app, in March. Animated GIFs are considerably simpler than the videos Sora generated, but the new feature brings a form of motion generation directly into ChatGPT without requiring a separate application.

OpenAI has not provided a specific date for when every ChatGPT account will receive GIF generation.
