#4973. Asian crypto exchanges batten down the hatches as hacks surge: Asia Express

Asian crypto exchanges are scrambling to tighten security on fears of a wave of potentially AI-assisted hacks, even as regional governments embrace tokenization and stablecoins.

India’s national cybersecurity agency warned that an AI-assisted threat actor has escalated its attacks on cryptocurrency exchanges and other financial businesses, stealing wallet keys and siphoning funds.

The Indian Computer Emergency Response Team (CERT-In) on Wednesday designated the threat actor as “CRAFTY FALCON” and explicitly identified crypto exchanges among affected businesses.

“The threat is active and ongoing. CSIRT-Fin continues to receive reports of successful attacks, including from NBFCs and fintech entities that had already received the earlier restricted advisories and had undertaken remediation,” said CERT-In.

The warning adds to evidence of hackers using AI tools against financial institutions. In October, cybersecurity firm CrowdStrike reported that an unidentified attacker used ARTEX, an AI penetration-testing tool also named in CERT-In’s advisory, alongside Claude Code and other AI models to breach South Korean financial firms and steal data.

South Korea’s Financial Services Commission has urged domestic crypto exchanges to increase the proportion of assets held in cold wallets after the $6.3 billion attack on Binance.

The FSC said it held an emergency meeting with other regulators, cybersecurity agencies and crypto service providers to review security measures.

It reviewed exchanges’ IT access controls, wallet withdrawal approval and signing procedures, and administrator privileges. It also urged providers to increase the proportion of assets held in offline cold wallets. But South Korean law already requires exchanges to hold at least 80% of users’ crypto assets in cold storage, with Upbit keeping 99.17% assets in cold wallets, followed by Bithumb at 94.76% and Coinone at 85%.

The Bank of Korea is surveying 31 financial institutions on how a major exchange shutdown, prolonged crypto downturn or losses at another firm could affect their operations.

One scenario asks how financial firms would be affected if South Korea’s largest crypto exchange by market share temporarily suspended operations following a major hack or similar incident.

Other questions address a steep fall in cryptocurrency prices followed by a sustained trading slump, and the potential fallout if another financial institution suffered heavy losses from a crypto-related incident.

Despite clear evidence of years of North Korea-linked hacking groups stealing billions from crypto exchanges, Pyongyang claims to have had no involvement in the $6.3 billion Binance hack and floated the idea it was actually the Americans.

“The FBI is trying to brand the DPRK as a ‘cyber criminal state’, claiming that it obtained information that a hacking organization ‘supported’ by the DPRK was involved in the incident,” said Kim Yo Jong said. “But the US assertion is an absurd rhetoric and nothing new.”

South Korea’s government has backed postponing tax on cryptocurrency gains by another two years to 2029. Judging on the troubled history of the tax, it would not be surprising if it is delayed again at that point.

Crypto.com will discontinue its consumer app services in Hong Kong on Feb. 1, 2027, while continuing to pursue regulatory approval for its institutional exchange.

A Crypto.com spokesperson told Cointelegraph that the company would discontinue its consumer app services in Hong Kong. The spokesperson said all affected customers had received advance notice and would receive instructions on closing their accounts and support to withdraw their funds.

Nearly three years after the local entity submitted an application for a license and the status remains “deemed-to-be-licensed” — which a transitional status rather than full regulatory approval.

The Hong Kong headquartered Animoca Brands has secured approval to manage investment funds for non-retail clients in Abu Dhabi.

The authorization, granted to subsidiary Animoca Asset Management (AD) Limited by Abu Dhabi Global Market’s Financial Services Regulatory Authority (FSRA), allows it to oversee funds that pool money from professional investors rather than the general public.

It expanding the asset management business of a company that began in mobile games before moving into blockchain gaming, rode the Metaverse wave and was a big player in venture investing.

Hong Kong has expanded its Project Ensemble pilot to allow round-the-clock settlement of tokenized transactions, as the city’s monetary regulator prepares to bring regulated stablecoins into trials involving tokenized government debt.

Hong Kong has recently officially introduced secondary market trading for SFC-authorised Tokenized Funds through licensed exchanges. Two China Asset Management money-market funds can now trade continuously via PantherTrade and the Futubull app.

Thailand’s securities regulator has filed a criminal complaint against BG Limited, the operator of cryptocurrency exchange Bitget, for allegedly operating without a license. Thailand’s Securities and Exchange Commission will block access to the website and app from December 5.

According to the SEC, Bitget offered crypto trading services between 2022 and 2025, charged trading fees and promoted its services in Thai through its websites and social media channels. The regulator said these activities violated Thailand’s digital asset business licensing requirements.

The regulator advised users to take action regarding assets held on the platform before the deadline.

Australia’s central bank raised its interest rate by 25 basis points to 4.85% on Tuesday, warning that inflation would stay elevated through much of 2027 as energy costs and AI-related investment added to price pressures.

The Reserve Bank of Australia’s monetary policy board voted 6–3 for the increase, with three members preferring to hold rates at 4.60%.

The bank pushed back its forecast for inflation to return to the top of its 2% to 3% target range until late 2027, around three quarters later than previously expected.

The Australian government’s decision to scrap the 50% capital gains discount for all investments in the May budget came as a big shock for crypto owners. The new scheme effectively doubles the taxable component of a gain, minus a small discount for inflation.

Some Aussie crypto holders are taking drastic measures — like Finder founder and crypto king Fred Schebesta who is relocating to Singapore which offers 0% CGT, stating the tax changes were a “killer blow.”But there are less dramatic methods to reduce CGT bills including setting up a self managed super fund (SMSF), a company to trade crypto at the company tax rate, or to hope the opposition will reverse the changes after the next election.

Japanese yen stablecoin JPYC marked its first anniversary with a circulating supply around 3 billion yen—one quarter of its internal target.

BlackRock, BlackRock Japan, SBI Holdings and Startale have signed an MOU to explore connecting tokenized money-market funds with SBI’s JPYSC yen stablecoin.

Ministry of Finance rules require cryptocurrency exchanges licensed in Vietnam to report security incidents and remediation plan within 24 hours

Five major banks—including HSBC, JPMorgan’s Kinexys, MUFG, Société Générale-Forge under BNP Paribas, and Standard Chartered—have launched the Global Layer One Organization (GL1) in Singapore. The non-profit aims to promote standards for tokenized financial markets.

#4972. Where to Buy Vintage Lamps (2026): 1stDibs, Etsy, and More

Listen—we’ve all fallen prey to the sort of lighting lethargy that keeps our hastily-purchased mass market table lamps on our bedside. A fluorescent task lamp might still hover over the couch, a garish flush mount shedding its harsh light. So why a vintage lamp? Because it indicates you thought about your lighting instead of going the route of a last-minute, add-to-cart impulse buy. This was deliberately searched and purchased. This was a choice made with historical design context in mind, whether it’s midcentury modern, Art Deco, or Hollywood Regency. A carefully considered piece of lighting shows your home decor chops—you’ve gone above and beyond the look of the room to consider the vibe of the room. A magnificent vintage piece communicates all that. We rounded up a few retailer options to find just the thing you need for a living room revamp, a ceiling light overhaul, or a lampshade upgrade.

We pull from 1stDibs regularly when shopping out our various home tours, where we can find the iconic works sprinkled throughout the homes of celebrities, designers, and celebrity designers. A Charlotte Perriand sconce indicates at least one substantial coffee book on the oeuvre of Le Corbusier is nearby, and a reissue of the 1960’s Gelato lamp by Italian designer Carlo Nason’s are just the tip of the iceberg. In other words: Design dorks will delight in this catalog of artifacts and iconic pieces.

Etsy is a go-to when you’re searching for a vibe rather than a specific model or designer. Try keyword searching for terms like “Art Deco” or “ginger jar lamp” and a trove of collectibles, like thrift-store-friendly finds and veritable antique gems, will surface in search. The online marketplace’s vast web of sellers means a library of vintage lighting that spans centuries and styles, often times for less than what you’d find at places like 1st Dibs and Chairish since sellers choose what they price for shipping. Contributor Lori Keong has had good luck buying pieces there from across the pond in the UK and even further afield in Slovenia, with lots of vintage sellers from the states like Philly’s Betsu and Chicago’s Spears Furnishings selling their wares on Etsy as well.

eBay brought me such things as this vintage Tarogo frog made from Ichiban rice paper that I passed up in a thrift store in Maine and thought about for months before it surfaced on the secondhand retailer. Now he sits proudly on an armoire overlooking my dining room. Which is to say: Every vintage table lamp that’s ever existed is somewhere in their backlog. Chinoiserie, vintage Noguchi lamps, kitschy conch shell lamps, Tiffany glass shades, the OG Pixar lamp—it’s all there.

Design Within Reach regularly re-issues iconic models in collaboration with the original manufacturers, giving second life to pieces like Verner Panton’s Panthella (1971), Herman Miller’s 1952 Cigar Lotus floor lamp, Greta Grossman’s 1947 Gräshoppa, and the Space Age-y Nessino table lamp which debuted in 1967, to name a few.

Rejuvenation’s vintage collection is part of a larger initiative to prevent waste. The brand’s Portland-based restoration studio team finds and restores over 3,000 antiques around the world every year to help keep them out of landfills. While the vintage pieces are one-of-a-kind finds, a large selection mirrors Rejuvenation’s midcentury modern-inspired offerings, like 20th-century brass lamps and designs with floral and gilt detailing.

Abask has some of the most beautiful antique Murano glassware lamps we’ve seen to date. Case in point: Albano Poli’s stained glass creation pictured below. The artist got his claim to fame for collaging colored glass across geometric lighting fixtures. Modern glass lamps like this one are easy to come by across Abask’s collections, as well as minimalist metal lamps and hand-painted ceramic lamps, if those better suit your style.

#4970. EGO Commercial expands ZTR line with mowers, blowers, and trimmers

EGO Power’s commercial group showed up at this week’s EQUIP Expo with a trio of new zero-turn riding mowers, a commercial-grade 780 CFM leaf blower, and a heavy-duty 16.5″ trimmer built for the 56V PGX battery system. And, with incentive programs designed to help landscapers make the switch to electric popping up all over, the timing couldn’t be better.

Visitors to the EGO Commercial booth this week were greeted with a trailer-load of new, professional-grade electric landscaping equipment, an expanded interchangeable battery ecosystem, and a connected equipment platform meant to help landscapers manage their energy needs now and prepare them for future growth. In other words: it was a lot.

“Our focus at EGO Commercial is developing products that deliver the performance professional landscapers demand so they can maximize productivity and minimize downtime,” said Tim Baker, vice president of product at EGO parent company, Chervon. “From professional-grade mowing and handheld equipment to scalable charging and fleet management, every component is designed to work together so landscaping businesses can get more from their battery investment across their entire operation.”

The official copy gets into some of the basic specs of the new EGO Commercial mowers, walking prospective buyers through the basics:

Commercial sit-down ZTR: Built for demanding commercial mowing, the sit-down ZTR delivers up to 44 gas-equivalent horsepower and mowing speeds of up to 12 mph, with durable 48-, 54- and 60-inch deck options. Powered by up to four removable, high-capacity batteries, the mower features an onboard 700-watt DC-to-DC charger, allowing professionals to recharge handheld EGO 56V ARC lithium batteries directly in the field.

Commercial stand-on ZTR: The stand-on ZTR delivers up to 44 gas-equivalent horsepower and mowing speeds of up to 12 mph, with durable 48-, 54- and 60-inch deck options. Equipped with up to four removable, high-capacity batteries, the mower features an onboard 700-watt DC-to-DC charger for convenient hand-held battery charging.

Commercial Compact Stand-On ZTR: Designed for crews and properties where maneuverability matters, the Compact Stand-On delivers up to 26 gas-equivalent horsepower with durable 32- and 36-inch deck options and mowing speeds of up to 9 mph.

Once you’ve leveled the turf and laid down your lawn stripes on your new EGO mower, they’ve got a pair of new, commercial-grade hand-held PGX power tools to help clean up the customers’ rough edges, too, with lightweight, easy-to-handle designs that ECO says help reduce operator fatigue, improve maneuverability, and make everyday tasks easier for you and your crew.

Commercial 780 CFM Blower: For precise cleanup and everyday applications, the blower combines controlled clearing performance with a lightweight, 4.8-pound bare-tool design to help reduce fatigue during repetitive tasks. Delivering air speeds up to 190 mph and 27 newtons of push force, it provides the maneuverability and control needed for detailed work, such as clearing grass clippings from mulch beds without disruption, as well as routine post-mowing cleanup.

16.5″ Front-Motor String Trimmer: Designed with operator comfort in mind, the 6.6-pound trimmer features a carbon-fiber shaft and direct-drive brushless motor for excellent balance and reduced vibration. With 28 CC gas-equivalent power, it delivers commercial cutting performance in a lightweight design that’s comfortable to handle throughout the workday.

The lineup is impressive enough on its own, but the big news here is the upgraded app-backed PGX battery management system that the company says allows businesses to configure charging based on the size and energy demands of their operations.

Designed for large, high-demand landscaping operations, EGO Commercial’s new 3200W PGX Hub offers 30 kWh (!) of overnight charging capacity and supports up to 48 (!!) three-port 56V (!!!) ARC-Lithium battery docks. A dedicated ZTR connection further expands the system’s versatility, allowing landscaping businesses to charge both the brand’s mowers and handheld tools with a single, “integrated” battery and charging system.

Perhaps more impressive than the power specs themselves is the fact that all of that is designed to work on a standard 120V outlet an a 15A circuit. “With PGX Commercial Charging solutions,” reads the copy, “no expensive electrical upgrades are required.”

EGO’s Commercial Fleet App gives landscaping crews another tool to keep their electric equipment working efficiently, letting fleet managers remotely monitor equipment location and usage, manage batteries and charging settings, and receive status alerts designed to maximize uptime.

#4968. 11 Best Love Seats for Small Spaces That Are Stylish (2026)

The most inviting living spaces have comfortable, functional seating options—but you don’t need a room big enough for a sprawling sectional to establish that welcoming vibe. Instead, turn to a small-space–friendly love seat, a compact sofa designed for two people, hence the name.

Even if you don’t live in a tiny apartment, a small sofa can add a romantic je ne sais quoi to a room, making a space feel more intimate, especially if you select a style with a curvy backrest that envelops guests like a hug. If you do have the space, pairing your love seat with a chaise or accent chairs can extend the footprint and add even more seating options while providing more opportunity to elevate the room’s design. One look at the vintage love seat that lives in Usher’s Atlanta home will make you a believer.

Here, find 11 designs, all between 55 and 70 inches wide, to accommodate all tastes, including the iconic mod Knoll womb settee and the more traditional leather love seat from Crate & Barrel.

Why settle for a traditional silhouette when you can have a bit of fun with your love seat? The West Elm Bogart style caught contributor Lori Keong’s eye because of its elegant bolstered back with rounded arms and a sculptural cutout base that conceals the legs. “No blocky, chubby legs or ugly hairpin appendages to stare at while I vacuum….what a concept!” she says in her review. Initially, she had some reservations about it being too stiff, classifying it as feeling similar to a medium-firm mattress. “It’s definitely firm and supportive but also not rock-hard, with just enough give—and no sag—to sleep or sit on comfortably.” Keong chose the alabaster basketweave, but if that color-upholstery combination doesn’t fit your space or style, now you can choose from 79 other options.

Serena & Lily wears the crown for coastal decor, but its offerings extend far beyond predictable patterns of sky pinstripes and gingham. With more than 125 upholstery options available—including linen, basketweave, cotton, denim, and more—in solid colors and patterns, the Spruce allows you to dress up the classic silhouette to fit a home of any style. If the slipcover is not your thing, the alternative is introducing exposed wood legs in four colorways. Regardless of your decor, the solid wood frame of this compact love seat is built to last.

For its boucle love seat, Quince uses performance fabric that is Oeko-Tex–certified, a designation that the textile in question is free of dangerous levels of harmful substances. Beneath the cozy, nubby fabric, foam cushions are supported by a hardwood frame, which is FSC-certified—a credential from the Forest Stewardship Council that specifies responsible wood sourcing. The combination of curvy, clean lines, and ivory color gives this two-seater an elevated elegance without a hefty price tag.

The Crate & Barrel Axis love seat comes in an astounding 45 leather options, including more traditional hues like caramel and bold burgundy and statement-making onyx. Silicone-based vegan materials are also available. The look, anchored by track arms and an angled back, is classic and can work just as well in an entryway as it would in a den.

The Pottery Barn Cameron power-reclining sofa may make you forget that you opted for a love seat at all. Once you hit the power button to recline and stretch out, you’ll forget all about this modular sofa’s compact stature. If your space can accommodate a few extra inches, consider adding a storage compartment between the seats for a spot to stash a mid-movie snack. Plus, the modular nature of this couch’s design means it can shift and grow with you, should you decide to place it elsewhere in your home or move to a new space altogether.

The Sabai Essential love seat looks like a hug. The low-profile seat with bench seating and plush back cushions practically begs for late-night reading sessions. Lounging is also sweeter when you know that the recycled polyester velvet is Oeko-Tex–certified as well as Global Recycled Standard 4.0-verified, a certification that affirms the content of recycled materials. And if you end up loving the seat too much, you can make use of the brand’s Repair Don’t Replace program that allows you to revive various components, like a single cushion or a scuffed sofa leg. Alternatively, if you prefer to refresh the upholstery du jour, simply swap it for a new slipcover at a fraction of the cost of a new love seat.

Joybird is the place to find a modern love seat, especially one that will go with your colorful wallpaper. The Amelia style is curvy with a twist: Its 1970s-inspired frame has a suave shape, but the cushions retain the expected rectangular configuration. Available in an eye-popping 91 colors, you should do yourself a favor and order the swatch sample box to help you choose. At 56.5 inches wide, it’s one of the smaller love seats on this list, making it ideal for teeny-tiny alcoves and nooks.

The AllModern Rae love seat comes in velvet and linen textures, giving you flexibility on whether you prefer a dressier or more casual style. Regardless of the upholstery choice, the channel stitching detail on the arms gives this compact love seat a contemporary look. The fabric is stain- and spill-resistant, but it is spot-clean–only. However, its reversible cushions let you hide a one-off oops. Also nice is that at just under 100 pounds, it’s a relatively easy sofa to move around by yourself. Toss in a few throw pillows, and you’ve got a whole new look.

Just looking at the CB2 Deseo makes us want to flop down and relax atop the two giant, pillow-like cushions filled with feathers and down. With a whopping 82 options for upholstery type and color, you can customize the love seat to fit into any room: Go for an opulent look with a rich merlot color, or tropical with the lsla Royal banana leaf linen-cotton for your sunroom.

Knoll earmarked a spot in the history of design with the Womb settee by Finnish architect Eero Saarinen, who said in 1948, “Today, more than ever, we need to relax.” Nearly 80 years later, the statement still rings true. It took Saarinen two years to make this chair, but from there, production didn't stop. Adding this piece to your furniture collection basically makes it an heirloom.

Take a page out of Brooklyn Decker and Andy Roddick’s Charlotte, North Carolina, entryway that features a small sofa with a back made of unexpected arches. The Lulu and Georgia Aimee Song Neve sofa surprises with its artfully sculpted seats that—at a medium on the firmness scale—are all about comfort. Among many upholstery and color options, it’s available in chunky boucle and garnet-and-blue stripe—a color combo that’s designer-approved.

#4967. NHTSA proposes cyan lights for robotaxis, following Mercedes/China

The NHTSA has proposed using cyan-colored lights to identify vehicles operating autonomously, along with other additions to get ready for autonomous vehicles.

As the autonomous vehicles start to spread on US roads, questions have come up about how to deal with these new driverless vehicles.

One question involves emergency response – not only do the vehicles need to be prepared to respond to emergency personnel, those personnel need to recognize when a car is operating autonomously. A police officer needs to know that nobody’s in the driver seat, or an emergency responder needs to know that if there’s nobody in the driver’s seat, that’s not necessarily because there’s a missing driver somewhere who needs rescue.

These are just some of the reasons one might want to know whether a car is operating autonomously, but as of yet, there is no standardized messaging to suggest that a vehicle is doing so.

This week, NHTSA proposed an answer. In a draft proposal released Friday, it suggested a whole new color for vehicle lamps, cyan, to signal autonomous operation.

The proposal was actually first put forward by Mercedes, alongside its DRIVE PILOT level 3 driver assist system. This is the first true hands-free system offered in the US, which allows drivers to forgo responsibility for the car under certain conditions, and let the car truly drive itself. This differs from systems like Tesla’s Full Self-Driving, which is actually only a “level 2” system, where the driver is still responsible when the system is on.

We tried Mercedes’ system out in Santa Monica traffic, and were impressed at how quickly we adjusted to our temporary robotic chauffer.

When we saw that system, we also saw Mercedes’ proposal for lighting: cyan lights around the exterior of the vehicle, denoting autonomous operation.

Unfortunately our demo was on a bright sunny day (a necessity to operate the level 3 system, which doesn’t run at night or in bad weather), so the lights didn’t pop as much as they might have in lower light, but you get the drift.

Mercedes trialed the system in California for two years, and it seems the NHTSA is happy with the results, since it is proposing to take the idea national (after input from the SAE, which also likes the idea of using cyan).

The new draft regulation would allow for optional inclusion of cyan lights, but would not require them. So, it’s entirely possible that automakers would decide to save the small cost that it would take to engineer and include these lights.

But by putting the idea out into the regulations, it gets everyone thinking about a future where this color might be required (and I could see that requirement coming quite quickly) – so automakers are likely to start thinking of where these lights would go, and including them, since LEDs don’t really cost anything anyway.

The regulation states that the lights should turn on when the car is operating autonomously, and turn off when it isn’t – they should never be on when a driver is actively driving the vehicle. They should all turn on and off simultaneously, and they should be visible from any angle, with markers on the front, sides and rear, all at least 15 inches off the ground.

China just announced essentially the same regulation earlier this week. The country released a draft standard that would take effect in 2028, is optional, and uses turquoise as its proposed color. This, too, was a reaction to the proliferation of lights on autonomous vehicles in China, and the government has asked automakers to stop doing their own thing and instead follow the new standardized system.

The NHTSA’s draft regulation covers several other aspects of autonomous vehicle operation, mostly by updating Federal Motor Vehicle Safety Standards (FMVSS) rules to allow some flexibility for certain operations that won’t be used by autonomous vehicles.

For example, cars are required to have a turn signal that turns off automatically after the steering wheel turns beyond a certain threshold and then returns back to neutral. That wouldn’t apply to cars without steering wheels, so the updated rules delete that requirement for autonomous cars. It also changes certain headlight requirements for more design flexibility.

While I’ve got a lot to say about the corrupt reality TV character running the DoT, and how he’s trying to raise your energy costs and kill children on bicycles, this autonomous vehicle regulation is a long time coming and is needed.

There are still some careerists in the NHTSA who take their job seriously, and are pushing regulations like this, and we can be thankful that not all of them have been forced out by the rampant corruption the US is seeing right now.

That said, the loosening of rules for autonomous vehicles is something we need to keep our eye on. Certain things, like allowing reasonable changes to FMVSS rules for things that really make no sense for an AV (like the turn signal change, or various required driver displays), are totally reasonable.

But it’s also hard not to look at this and say, well, could the guy who has given hundreds of millions in bribes to try to cancel investigations against him and wants to sell you an unusable car without a steering wheel be behind any of this?

NHTSA would be better off just shutting down and copy-paste regulations from EU on vehicle safety and China on autonomy. Both require more stringent safety and it would align the US with the larger markets.

Sadly, the US regulators are no longer independent thanks to the rampant political corruption and they are no longer sufficiently staffed to develop and implement regulations. Outsource development and become an agency that enforces the regulations developed in the more advanced markets.

After all, that steering-wheel-less car was subject to an immediate NHTSA investigation when it was suddenly put into service without forewarning, with the NHTSA pointing out myriad problems. Changing the rules after the fact to accompany a vehicle that already violated them doesn’t set great precedent.

So… we can be happy for the adaptation to new technology, but still need to ensure that safety is the North Star, rather than Mr. Musk’s fantasies. We have no specific reason in mind to oppose this specific regulation, but an environment so rife with corruption does not foster trust with the general populace, so here we are.

The rule will soon be posted for public comment, for a 30 day period, under docket NHTSA-2026-1519 at www.regulations.gov.

#4964. American Bitcoin’s $126M profit masks mining pressure as rivals turn to AI

The Eric Trump-backed miner reported $126 million in quarterly net income, largely driven by gains on its Bitcoin holdings, despite rising mining costs and shrinking margins.

American Bitcoin reported a quarterly net income of $126 million on Wednesday, but rising mining costs and falling profits from mining raise questions about its long-term strategy as major rivals pivot towards artificial intelligence.

The Eric Trump-backed company’s profit was driven by a $197.3 million noncash gain on its Bitcoin (BTC) holdings. Meanwhile, mining revenue fell 5.5% to $63.4 million from the previous quarter, while the cost to mine one BTC rose about 15% to $42,100.

Excluding gains from its Bitcoin holdings, American Bitcoin would have reported an operating loss of approximately $5.5 million, according to its financial statements. The company also recorded a separate $64.3 million loss on derivatives.

“Despite seasonal power cost pressures, we continued mining Bitcoin at a structural discount to spot,” American Bitcoin CEO Mike Ho said in Wednesday’s earnings announcement.

Earnings per share stood at $1.73, beating analysts’ consensus estimate of $0.26, according to Yahoo Finance data.

Bitcoin was trading at $76,119, down 5% over the past 24 hours as of 8:50 p.m. UTC, according to CoinGecko data. Shares of American Bitcoin (ABTC), meanwhile, closed Thursday down about 15% at $5.31.

The results highlight an interesting divide among Bitcoin miners: while American Bitcoin continues mining and holding BTC, competitors like Riot Platforms, MARA Holdings and Core Scientific are investing in AI to find new sources of revenue.

David Gamble, founder of asset management company Enigma Holdings, which focuses on Bitcoin mining and AI infrastructure, said American Bitcoin’s reported profit does not tell the full story of its mining business.

“Ultimately, mining Bitcoin below its market price doesn’t automatically mean you’re creating shareholder value,” Gamble told Cointelegraph.

American Bitcoin’s gross mining profit fell to $25.8 million from $33 million in the previous quarter. Its reported mining margin dropped to 41% from 49%, leaving the company with a smaller share of mining revenue after direct costs.

The company’s $63.4 million in quarterly revenue also fell short of analysts’ estimates of $74.35 million, according to Yahoo Finance data, a gap of nearly 15%.

The company mined 891 BTC during the quarter, down from 932 BTC in the previous three months. It also said higher electricity prices were the main reason mining costs increased.

“I don’t necessarily think their accumulation strategy is flawed, but I do think it raises questions about long-term sustainability,” Gamble said, pointing to increasing mining difficulty and the need to keep investing in newer and more efficient technology to compete.

Compared with the same period last year, revenue was nearly unchanged, falling from $64.2 million to $63.4 million. Reported profit, however, jumped from $3.5 million to $126 million, largely reflecting gains on the company’s Bitcoin holdings.

Related: Bitcoin miners face widening AI financing costs as lenders scrutinize deals

American Bitcoin’s strategy comes as competitors like Riot Platforms, MARA Holdings and Core Scientific rapidly expand into AI.

Riot is building large AI data centers, according to its latest earnings. Meanwhile MARA is expanding into AI while continuing to mine Bitcoin. Core Scientific, on the other hand, has already shifted much of its business toward AI data centers.

American Bitcoin, by contrast, continues building its BTC reserves, holding 8,427 BTC at the end of September, up from 3,418 BTC a year earlier.

“The real competitive advantage in this industry is shifting toward energy infrastructure,” Gamble said. “It’s no longer just about how much Bitcoin you can mine or how much hashrate you can deploy.”

Gamble also explained that Bitcoin miners and AI companies are competing for the same power supply, and AI data centers could make more money than Bitcoin mining in some areas.

“That doesn’t mean every mining facility can simply transition into AI,” he said. “You still have to account for depreciation, equipment replacement, financing costs, and the opportunity cost of deploying that capital elsewhere.”

#4963. Jaylen Brown Is Keeping His Boston Skyrise Apartment—and Its 18th-Century Harp Too

Jaylen Brown has an 18th-century harp sitting in his living room, and that harp rules. Having a harp in your home as a newly 30-year-old man, with a professional harpist who appears when the mood strikes said 30-year-old man, is an outstanding use of free will.

“Most of the time I use my harp, it’s just me and my chef,” Brown says. A memory raced through my head: me at the end of my twenties, with a harp (broken) in my unit. And how central it would be to the social hours of 10 p.m. to 4 a.m.

Brown’s harp is not that. “It’s almost like therapy sessions,” he says.

Jaylen Brown with his cat Forky in the speakeasy of his Boston apartment design by Linda Zarifi of Zarifi Design. The project construction is by Core Group Boston, and the art advisory is by Miranda Girard. The sofa is by Charles Zana from The Invisible Collection and the ottoman is also from The Invisible Collection.

The artwork in the speakeasy is by Francisco Valverde and flanked with sconces by Apparatus. The curtain fabric is by Dedar, and the star ceiling lights are by UFO Lighting.

The wallcovering in the speakeasy is by Gregorius Pineo.

He’s a different dude in a way that makes him singular, both as a member of the National Basketball Association and as a young and famous Black man in the United States of America.

On July 1, 2026, hundreds of television talkers said “the basketball world has been turned upside down,” because Brown’s years as a Celtic were over. After nine years as half of Boston’s dynamic duo with Jayson Tatum, he was traded to Philadelphia. “It’s no beef,” Jaylen politely told reporters after a victory, beard absolutely glistening. “Might be a little lamb chops and gravy. He should stay ready at all times.” 23 days later, Brown’s new teammate became LeBron James, creating one of the strongest starting fives in NBA history. You love to see someone find a nice new gig. But as for where he rests his head, Brown held on to his place in Boston.

In the foyer, the walls are painted in Ashland Slate by Benjamin Moore. The custom table holds Brown’s championship trophy.

In the living area, the Hamilton sofa by Minotti surrounds coffee tables by Carlyle Collective. The floor lamp is by Tom Dixon and the harp is antique.

“I grew up here, age 19 to 29,” he says. “I developed here in Boston as a young man.” The feeling is mutual; October 24, his birthday, was named Jaylen Brown Day by the city. But the crib itself is doing a lot of the pulling. “There’s been other places, but this one–I created it, it was crafted by my vision,” he says. “This one felt the most like home.”

He hired a team to bring his dreams to life. Linda Zarifi, of Zarifi Design, was his interior designer and collaborator. “The first thing he said was ‘I don’t want a dining table,’” Zarifi says. “And I’m like, ‘Where are you going to…eat?’”

The answer was a 22-foot banquette, built for most home activities. “I wanted to open the space up. I wanted gold. I wanted a see-through wine cellar. She went and found it all,” Brown says.

The custom dining banquette is upholstered in Crescendo Suede by Kravet and the glass and metal tables are also custom by Jamie Cumming. The TG-10 Sling dining chairs are by Gratz Industries and the wall sconces are by Gubi. Inside the frame on the wall is a proclamation from the city of Boston for Jaylen Brown Day.

Brown in the dining 22-foot banquette playing chess.

The result is a 4,000 square-foot apartment overlooking Boston’s Financial District. Brown purchased the downtown space at the end of 2023 and reworked it over the last two years. Outside, the apartment is a brief 15 minute walk from the TD Garden, home to Brown’s former team, the Boston Celtics. Inside, Italian marble bathroom floors, 24-karat fixtures that Brown calls “jewelry for the bathroom,” a custom suede pool table, a party-ready speakeasy, and that see-through wine cellar.

There’s also the medical hyperbaric chamber one step from his bed, one of the truest signs that he is a single man.

Jaylen is figuring it out in real time, and he’s letting us watch and listen.

The trophy case in the speakeasy is a custom design by Zarifi and fabricated by Jamie Cumming of Loki Custom Furniture.

A shelf in the speakeasy holds a collection of Brown’s memorabilia.

A chandelier by John Pomp hangs over the bar area with stools by Blackman Cruz upholstered in a leather by Holly Hunt.

Kashmir barstools from Resident tuck under the kitchen counter. The flushmounts are from RH and the kitchen cabinets are painted in Cheating Heart by Benjamin Moore.

They say he’s not new to this. In 2020, five days after George Floyd was murdered, he drove 15 hours from Boston to our hometown of Atlanta to lead a march. The same guy who took the swooshes off his Nikes and now has his own shoe company (with custom closets by Molteni&C to house his own collection). And the same guy who uses this home to stream with Lil Baby and to display art and artifacts dedicated to his family.

“The things that he would splurge on are the things that mattered most to him,” Zarifi says. There’s the commissioned portrait in cracked glass by Simon Berger of his grandmother in the foyer. “For him, his flex was his harp.”

Brown seated on a bench in the primary bedroom. The custom bed is upholstered in a fabric by Mokum Textiles. The pendants by Apparatus hang over custom nightstands flanking the bed.

“I had them recreate the bedding and the comforter of the Four Seasons,” Brown says. The bedding is custom from Crem & Co. Brown’s hyperbaric chamber is beside the bed.

“I love the tempered glass,” Brown says. The closet design is the Glis Closet System by Molteni&C. The pendant light is by Henge.

The custom vanity in the primary bathroom is flanked with sconces by Articolo. The fittings are by Dornbracht.

The tub and counters are topped in a Pietra Grey stone and the ceiling is painted Ashland Slate by Benjamin Moore.

The Sally armchair in the primary bedroom is by Minotti and covered in Holly Hunt fabric.

He hasn’t taken lessons yet, good for no more than an impassioned glissando. What he does do, however, is soak in the sounds from Li Shan Tan, the principal harp for the New Haven Symphony Orchestra, who plucks from his palace. “From time to time, when she is not around, I go and I try to recreate some sounds.”

#4962. Here’s what happened in crypto today

Need to know what happened in crypto today? Here is the latest news on daily trends and events impacting Bitcoin price, blockchain, DeFi, Web3 and crypto regulation.

Today in crypto, Bitcoin fell below $76,000 as US stocks reversed their post-election gains, France’s National Assembly rejected a budget revenue bill containing proposed crypto tax measures and Democrats threatened investigations into President Donald Trump’s crypto ventures.

Bitcoin (BTC) fell below $76,000 on Thursday as risk assets abruptly reversed their US midterm elections rally. After making swift gains on the back of a Democrat sweep of the US House of Representatives and Senate, equities saw sharp selling on Thursday as traders rotated out of tech.

Crypto markets liquidated nearly $500 million in positioning in the first hour of the US trading session, per data from CoinGlass, with 24-hour total liquidations nearing $850 million.

Now targeting $76,000, BTC/USD approached the bottom of its one-month trading range. The pair risked losing support at the 50-week simple moving average (SMA), currently at $75,733.

In his latest analysis, crypto trader Rekt Capital noted that Bitcoin was repeating behavior from May, when a period of rangebound moves around $80,000 subsequently gave way to a breakdown and new macro lows of $57,000 in late June.

The French National Assembly rejected the 2027 state budget’s revenue section late Wednesday, removing crypto-related amendments from the version of the bill sent to the Senate.

The Assembly rejected the revenue section by a vote of 458–0 at first reading, with two abstentions, according to the official voting record. The revenue section covers government income, such as taxes.

After the rejection, the government will send the Senate the original budget text, without the crypto-related amendments adopted by lawmakers.

The proposals could still be reintroduced in the Senate and would need to survive the remaining budget process to become law.

Faustine Fleuret, global head of public affairs at lending protocol Morpho, told Cointelegraph before the vote that rejection of the revenue section could “result in the rejection of the entire bill in the National Assembly, including provisions adopted by way of amendments.”

Democratic lawmakers said Big Tech executives should expect a wave of subpoenas, along with investigations into US President Donald Trump’s business circle when the new Congress takes office in January.

As Democrats secured control of Congress in a sweeping victory on Tuesday, Democratic lawmakers were already firing warning shots at the Trump family and tech executives close to the US president.

“They are going to need to plant new forests for all of the subpoenas we’ll be printing,” said US Senator Ed Markey, who sits on the Senate Commerce Committee, referring to Big Tech executives.

US Representative Robert Garcia, the expected incoming House Oversight Committee chairman, said he plans to take on “the Trump Crime Family, corporate corruption, DHS and ICE abuses, and the cover-up of the Epstein files,” adding a two-word warning: “Lawyer up.”

The warnings follow a mid-term election campaign in which Democrats challenged Big Tech’s growing political influence and the rapid expansion of AI, raising concerns about data centers’ demands on electricity, safeguards for AI products and tech executives’ ties to Trump.

#4959. Chinese EVs found back door into Western car markets – at 40% off MSRP

The dumb oil war in Iran is driving up the cost of just about everything, including new cars. That’s giving Chinese EVs a huge advantage over Western “legacy” car brands already lagging in tech and struggling with affordability – and those legacy brands about to have a whole new to them problem on their hands: USED Chinese EVs.

Western automakers have leaned on protectionist trade policies to keep cheaper Chinese rivals at bay, placing strict limits on the number of new Chinese cars allowed in and hefty tariffs on new battery-electric vehicles (BEVs) are as high as 45.3% in the EU. That supply limitation is, of course, ratcheting up demand for the high-tech, low-cost Chinese machines, and buyers in Europe and Canada are finding that used Chinese EVs – which offer comparable tech to the newest Western EVs, a price tag up to 40% lower than their already low MSRPs, and which are not subject to the same protectionist import duties – are a workable alternative.

According to data shared by importers/exporters China EV Marketplace (CEVM), pre-owned vehicle sales to EU customers grew 280% year over year, with sales climbing to 7,900 units through September.

“Tariffs have already weakened demand for new Chinese BEVs among our European customers. We expect the hybrid import cap to put pressure on new PHEV sales too,” CEVM COO Jakub Geršl told CarNewsChina. “Pre-owned cars are exempt from the additional BEV tariffs and from the new hybrid (PHEV) quota. That is why we are making pre-owned vehicles the main focus of our European business.”

CEVM has more than 2,600 used Chinese EVs on its website as I type this, and their inventory is refreshed almost daily with fresh pre-owned examples of cars from brands like BYD, Geely, Zeekr, and others – and the company claims its prices are about 40% below the original price (MSRP) on average.

“Lower new-car prices put pressure on pre-owned prices as well,” Geršl adds. “Our Shanghai-based team searches for those opportunities. We regularly find cars with around 6,000 km on the clock priced 40% below their original Chinese list price.”

That price reduction is driven by a number of factors, including the relentless march of technological progress, the flood of new and updated EVs coming out of Chinese car brands, and a burgeoning domestic Chinese car culture that values newness above all else. If you’re reading between the lines there, I think you’ll find that that means more low-mileage, low-price, high-tech, and high-value Chinese EVs are coming to the West – and probably a lot sooner than brands like VW, who are struggling to compete as it is, would want.

And that’s the real problem – whether you agree with him or not, Elon Musk will tell you that technology and markets move faster than legislation, and once consumers go electric, precious few of them ever go back to gas or diesel, regardless of what that dude filming himself wearing PitVipers in the drivers’ seat of his pickup and your racist uncle on Facebook are telling you.

My concern would be lack of service facilities. At some point every car, even EVs, needs to go to the service center for something, and the more technology-laden the vehicle, the more important it is to have service centers that know what they're doing. I wouldn't count on the local Chevy dealer being able to deal with a mandarin software stack.

Let us know if you agree, and if you’d give a used Chinese EV a chance if one showed up at your local Carvana, in the comments.

#4958. This Picture-Perfect Suburban Kitchen Is an Argument for Maxed-Out Storage, Smart Appliances, and Custom Furniture

The house had everything a young family could want: classic Georgian architecture, a picturesque setting in East Glenview, Illinois, and a neighborhood where kids ride bikes and golden retrievers lounge in the sunshine. For a Chicago couple with three young sons, it checked nearly every box. The kitchen, however, was another story. Its awkward layout, dated appliances, and surprisingly scant storage made it feel ill-equipped for the demands of a family of five.

Fortunately, the problem was one that could be solved. Elizabeth Smolcich, principal interior designer of Glenview-based firm Mason & Brass, saw its potential. “We knew we’d have to do a heavy lift to make the kitchen feel like ours,” says homeowner Marie Gould. “Elizabeth had a vision for it that made it much more functional and kid-friendly for our family.”

Kitchen layout, before and after. A wider doorway improves the flow between kitchen and living room, while removal of a corner booth created space for a larger island and the potential for more seating.

Homeowner Marie Gould with her sons in the renovated kitchen

Smolcich started by widening the opening between the kitchen and living room just enough to let Gould easily eyeball what her boys, ages seven, six, and three, are up to while she’s cooking. And removing an awkwardly positioned corner booth, one that was too small to seat four comfortably but obtrusive enough to impede the 450-square-foot room’s flow, gave the designer enough space to expand and center a new kitchen island.

This island, topped with smoky quartzite, provides plenty of counter space for prepping meals or rolling out dough, and even has enough room for the boys to perch on when they want a close-up view of the action. Above the island, Smolcich installed schoolhouse-style fluted glass pendants, which the couple loved for their timeless appeal. The most significant addition came in the form of a custom banquette that looks built into the island but is actually a movable bench. The family slides this upholstered piece to one side of their dining table, which can expand from 66 inches to 96 inches, when they’re hosting a crowd. (They’re expecting more than 40 friends and family for Thanksgiving this year.)

This is a family that loves to entertain, says the designer. A double oven, then, was a necessity, as was a serious range that allows Gould to sear a steak with restaurant-quality char. Gould discovered the ultimate time-saving mom hack with her smart SKS range, which features a rapid preheat function and can boil a pot of water for pasta in just 60 seconds. It has both an induction cooktop to prevent tiny hands from burns as well as four gas burners that satisfy even the pickiest of eaters.

Two ovens were a necessity for the homeowners’ busy household. Brizo Rook faucets and Visual Comfort Parkington sconces, both in brass, warm up the space.

The designer flanked the stove with open shelving to keep oils and seasonings within easy reach. The dual gas and induction SKS range features Smart Knob technology.

“They’re big on texture,” says Gould of her family. “They love a sear and a proper crust, which is no problem with this.” (That said, she says she personally appreciates the range’s sous vide capability best, although the SKS Smart Knobs, with LED screens for each knob to monitor heat levels and time remaining, are a close second.)

Over the range Smolcich installed hidden, integrated built-in storage to hold Gould’s go-to oils and seasonings. “We took advantage of any and all real estate,” explains the designer. “We can make anything beautiful. That’s not the hard part of what we do. The hard part is making it wildly functional. If you feel like you have to go out of your way to retrieve something, you’re never going to love it.”

Designer Elizabeth Smolcich’s mood board of furniture, lighting, and surfaces

Hence the dual SKS dishwashers staggered from the Kohler prep sink in the island to the main Kohler sink (with a custom cutting board) on the perimeter; the pantry wall (painted Benjamin Moore Black Iron); an appliance garage (housing Gould’s toaster, blenders, mixer, and coffee maker); and a beverage bar with a 24-inch second fridge stocked with her kids’ juice boxes.

Prominently displayed is a treasured side-by-side picture frame with a photo of Gould’s parents baking with her oldest son next to a handwritten copy of their family’s banana bread recipe.

“That’s what this kitchen is to them,” says Smolcich. “In a few years, those boys will be sitting at that banquette doing their homework. We took this big house with a kitchen built for just a few people and made it one for a family.”

The beverage bar includes an under-the-counter refrigerator where the kids can grab their juice boxes.

#4954. Volkswagen’s new electric Jetta offers 285 miles of range for under $12K

Volkswagen is offering its Chinese-market Jetta M6 electric sedan with nearly 290 miles of CLTC-rated range for less than $12,000 (USD), and if that’s not what “der people’s wagen” should be all about, I don’t know what is.

Launched as a joint venture between FAW and Volkswagen Group, the Jetta brand’s first battery electric car is finally here – and it is a genuinely accessible, capable, and kinda decent-looking family sedan in the same way VW’s original Type I was when it was first developed in the 1940s.

The new Jetta M6 was launched earlier this in three variants: the 465 Pro, 555 Max and 555 Ultra; each priced at 79,800 yuan (~$11,800), 89,800 yuan and 99,800 yuan, respectively, with the model numbers corresponding to the car’s estimated CLTC range in kilometers (which maths out around ~285 and 345 miles, if you’re curious).

Built on FAW’s Jiesi architecture, the M6 measures 4,806 mm long and 1,868 mm wide, putting it right in that sedan sweet spot between the Civic/Corolla and Accord/Camry crews, and within a few mm this way and that of the Tesla Model 3, so it should have plenty of room for young professionals and families – but the M6′ real selling point is its tech.

Inside, the Jetta M6 offers an 8.88″ driver display along with a big 15.6″ central touchscreen that integrates Chinese AI assistants Doubao and DeepSeek and ships with Apple CarPlay baked in. Advanced ADAS with, “intelligent cruise assistance, automatic emergency braking, and automated parking” is standard.

The entry-level “465 Pro” version of the Jetta M6 comes equipped with 42.9 kWh LFP battery and a 113 kW (~150 hp) electric motor driving the front wheels, a combination the company says is good for a 8.3-second sprint to 100 km/h (62 mph). Stepping up to the 555 versions gets you a larger 51.9 kWh battery and more powerful 145 kW (~195 hp) motor that cuts that sprint to 6.8 seconds.

Both the 42.9 and 51.0 kWh batteries are lithium iron phosphate deals supplied by CALB, with charging from 20% to 80% taking about 30 minutes.

There are parts of the US where a car like this would be perfect -- it's got enough range for the daily commute and short weekend day trips. Not for everyone, but for enough people that it would be a big deal.

Would those features and performance numbers be enough to get you to look at something like this in the US, even at twice the price? It seems to me that North America’s VW dealers need all the help they can get, and a volume leader like this with a “back to basics” ad campaign that plays on a different kind of nostalgia – nostalgia for a time when people could afford a Jetta, maybe? – could be a winner. Let us know what you think in the comments.
